I can only say: If I didn’t _see_ that my arm had moved after I, with my face turned away, was convinced that I had moved it, I would be confused and would probably trust my _eyes_. Seeing can certainly _teach_ me whether I executed the intended movement accurately, e.g., whether I reached the position that I wanted to reach; the _feeling_ could not do that. I certainly feel that I am moving, and I can also judge approximately, from the feeling, _how_, but I simply _know_ what movement I made, without it being possible to speak of a _sense-datum_ of the movement, of an immediate inner image of the movement. And when I say “I simply _know_…”, here “knowing” means something like “being able to say” and is not, perhaps, another kind of inner depiction.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#282.3+283.1) <span class="fac">[282\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/282.webp),[283\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/283.webp)</span> {#ts-229-282-3--283-1}

<span class="series-number">1059</span> “In order to be able to say that the feeling teaches me where my arm is now, or how far I am moving it, one would have to have associated feelings and movements with each other. One would have to be able to say: ‘If I have this feeling…, then my arm is, according to experience, _there_’. Or also: One would have to have a criterion for the identity of the feelings in addition to the one of the executed movement.” But is this condition fulfilled for _seeing_, if it has any meaning at all? Well, one can represent a visual image, e.g., graphically. But one cannot give someone, or oneself, the feeling that is supposed to be characteristic of bending one's arm by 30<sup>˚</sup>, without actually bending one's arm. Bend your arm a little! What do you feel? – A tension, or something similar, here and there, and mainly the rubbing of my sleeve. – Do it again! Was the feeling the same? _Approximately_. Approximately in the same places. Does _this_ feeling always accompany this movement, can you say? No. And yet, something about this argument still doesn’t quite sit right with me.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#283.2) <span class="fac">[283\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/283.webp)</span> {#ts-229-283-2}

<span class="series-number">1060</span> Imagine that certain movements produce sounds and we now say that we recognize how far we have moved our arm by the sound that rings out. That would be possible. (Playing a scale on the piano.) But what prerequisites would have to be met for this? It would not be enough, for example, that sounds accompany the movements; nor would it be enough that they are similar for similar movements. It would also not be enough to say: the sound _must_ have the _same_ quality for the same movements, since it is the only sense-datum by which we can recognize the size of the movement.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#283.3) <span class="fac">[283\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/283.webp)</span> {#ts-229-283-3}

<span class="series-number">1061</span> But isn’t there, after all, a kind of private ostensive definition for movements and the like? I bend a finger, for example, and remember the sensation. Someone now tells me, “I will produce certain sensations in your finger in a certain way, but without it moving; tell me when it is _the_ one you are having now when you bend your finger.” Could I not, for my own use, call this sensation “E,” use my memory as a criterion for identity, and now say “Yes, that is E again,” etc.?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#283.4) <span class="fac">[283\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/283.webp)</span> {#ts-229-283-4}

<span class="series-number">1062</span> It would then also be conceivable that I recognize the sensation, and that it occurs _without_ the accompaniment of the conviction: that the movement has taken place – without the sense of movement.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#283.5) <span class="fac">[283\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/283.webp)</span> {#ts-229-283-5}

<span class="series-number">1063</span> I can certainly, for example, raise my knee several times in a row and say that I had the same sensation each time: not as if I have this sensation _every_ time I raise my knee, nor as if I can recognize the movement by the sensation, but merely: I had the same sensation, produced by the movement, three times in this series of knee movements. “The same” here, of course, means the same as “appearing the same.”


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#284.1) <span class="fac">[284\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/284.webp)</span> {#ts-229-284-1}

<span class="series-number">1064</span> “I have had the same sensation three times” – this describes a process in my private world. But how does the other person know what I mean? What do I mean by “same” in such a case? Does he rely on the fact that I use the word here as I always do? But what is, in this case, the usual, _analogous_ use? No, this difficulty is not a mere quibble; he doesn't _really_ know, cannot know, what the same objects are in this case.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#284.2) <span class="fac">[284\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/284.webp)</span> {#ts-229-284-2}

<span class="series-number">1065</span> The example of the motor roller with the motor in the roller is really much better and deeper than I explained. Because, when someone presented the design to me, I immediately saw that it could not work, because one could roll the roller from the outside, even if the ‘motor’ was not in operation; but I didn't see that it was a rigid construction and not a machine at all. And here there is a close analogy to the case of the private indicative definition. For also in that case there is, as it were, a direct and an indirect way of realizing the impossibility.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#284.3) <span class="fac">[284\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/284.webp)</span> {#ts-229-284-3}

<span class="series-number">1066</span> I named this sensation of movement with “E.” For the other person, it is now the one I had during this movement. But for me? Does “E” now mean something else? – Well, for me it means _this_ sensation. – But which one is this? because I pointed to my sensation a minute ago – how can I now point to _it_ again?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#284.4) <span class="fac">[284\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/284.webp)</span> {#ts-229-284-4}

<span class="series-number">1067</span> But suppose, for example, that someone makes a series of arm movements and says, “The sensation I have in my leg now, I call it ‘E<sub>1</sub>’,” and so on. Later, on various occasions, he says, “Now I have E<sub>3</sub>.” And so on. – Such utterances could be important; if, for example, we observe certain physiological correlates to the sensations and can thus draw conclusions from his utterances.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#284.5+285.1) <span class="fac">[284\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/284.webp),[285\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/285.webp)</span> {#ts-229-284-5--285-1}

<span class="series-number">1068</span> If it is true that we do not judge the type and size of a limb's movement by the _feeling_, how would a person differ from us in whom it is the case? Well, one could easily imagine that someone has different strengths or different types of sensations of pain with different movements. He would, for example, say, “I feel this stabbing pain when I bend my arm at about 90<sup>˚</sup>.”


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#285.2) <span class="fac">[285\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/285.webp)</span> {#ts-229-285-2}

<span class="series-number">1069</span> Imagine someone who can determine the depth of a spring with the dowsing rod, and according to the force it exerts. He learned it _this_ way: He walked over springs of different depths and memorized the force. (This could have been determined with a spring scale.) He associated the force with the depth and now infers the depth from the force. This could happen in such a way that he indicates the force in kg, for example, and then goes over to the depth, perhaps even according to a table. But it is also possible that he knows no other measure of the force than the depth of the spring. After some practice, he can correctly state the depth. If a force is exerted on the rod, for example, by weights, he will now also say, “This pulls like a spring of such and such depth.”


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#285.3) <span class="fac">[285\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/285.webp)</span> {#ts-229-285-3}

<span class="series-number">1070</span> But it could be that he is able to correctly indicate the depth of a spring by the force of the rod, but not to correctly estimate the force of the rod. I mean this: it could be that water at different depths under different circumstances exerts the same force; and this dowser now says, for example, “This spring is deeper than the previous one, it pulls more weakly” – and he is right: the spring is indeed deeper, but the force, measured with the spring scale, was the same, and he had not correctly memorized _it_. – – Should I then say in this case that he judges the depth according to the force?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#285.4) <span class="fac">[285\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/285.webp)</span> {#ts-229-285-4}

<span class="series-number">1071</span> He will perhaps say: “This move is that of a spring at a certain depth…”, by studying this move, as one weighs a weight in one’s hand. Perhaps, however, he says: “I cannot judge this move; the water is at a certain depth…” In this (latter) case, one would not say that he judges the depth according to the move. (At least not ‘consciously’.)


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#285.5+286.1) <span class="fac">[285\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/285.webp),[286\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/286.webp)</span> {#ts-229-285-5--286-1}

<span class="series-number">1072</span> Suppose one says that he judges how much he has bent his arm by the strength of a pressure sensation in his elbow. This means: When it reaches a certain strength, he recognizes from it that the arm is bent to that degree. Or what else should it mean: that he judges the degree of the bend according to the degree of the pressure sensation?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#286.2) <span class="fac">[286\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/286.webp)</span> {#ts-229-286-2}

<span class="series-number">1073</span> I mean: How does one know that one is judging something according to this sensation? – Is it enough for this that when estimating, one directs his attention to the sensation?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#286.3) <span class="fac">[286\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/286.webp)</span> {#ts-229-286-3}

<span class="series-number">1074</span> If you now say that it is necessary for this that one can indicate: “If the pressure is _so_ strong, then my arm is bent by 90<sup>˚</sup>” – then the ‘_so_’ must be specifiable with respect to the strength. Otherwise, it means that one judges the bending based on the sensation of pressure, at most that one cannot judge the bending if one feels _no_ (or only a very weak) sensation of pressure. (For example, if one is anesthetized.)


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#286.4) <span class="fac">[286\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/286.webp)</span> {#ts-229-286-4}

<span class="series-number">1075</span> There are therefore different cases. One can say that one judges the bend according to the pressure or pain sensation, and in a way listens to this sensation; but otherwise, one cannot specify the degree of the sensation in any way. – Or there can be two independent statements of the degree of the sensation and the bend.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#286.5) <span class="fac">[286\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/286.webp)</span> {#ts-229-286-5}

<span class="series-number">1076</span> “If I feel the pressure so strongly, then…” – Doesn’t that make sense? Someone might even say that he has an entire scale of pressure sensations. I can imagine that. Only, that would be as little a real scale as the picture of a thermometer is a thermometer. Although it does have considerable similarity with it in some respects.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#286.6+287.1) <span class="fac">[286\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/286.webp),[287\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/287.webp)</span> {#ts-229-286-6--287-1}

<span class="series-number">1077</span> I give the rules of a game. The other person, in complete accordance with these rules, makes a move whose possibility I had not foreseen, and which disrupts the game in the way that I wanted it to. I must now say: “I gave bad rules”; I must change my rules, or perhaps supplement them. So, did I already have an image of the game in advance? In a certain sense: yes! It was, for example, possible that I did not foresee that a quadratic equation does not have to have real solutions. The rule therefore leads me to something, about which I say: “I did not expect this image; I always imagined a solution like this:…”


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#287.2) <span class="fac">[287\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/287.webp)</span> {#ts-229-287-2}

<span class="series-number">1078</span> How would it be if one said: “Not every system of rules determines a calculus”? As an example, one would give division by 0. Suppose we imagine an arithmetic in which it is allowed and from which it can be proven that every number is equal to the other.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#287.3) <span class="fac">[287\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/287.webp)</span> {#ts-229-287-3}

<span class="series-number">1079</span> When children play trains, – should I say, when a child imitates the locomotive, is it seen as a locomotive by another child? In the game, it is _taken as_ a locomotive. Imagine I showed an adult the shape 🖵, and asked “What does it remind you of,” and he answered “A locomotive” – does that mean he _saw_ it as a locomotive? I take that as the typical game of “seeing something as something,” when someone says “Now I see it as this, now as that.” When he knows different aspects, and that _independently_ of any use of the thing being looked at. So, I would like to say that I do not consider the use of the picture as a sign that it is seen in this way or that way.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#287.4) <span class="fac">[287\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/287.webp)</span> {#ts-229-287-4}

<span class="series-number">1080</span> Would a child understand what it means to see the table ‘as a table’? It learns: “This is a table, this is a bench,” etc., and it completely masters a language-game without the slightest hint that this involves an aspect.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#287.5) <span class="fac">[287\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/287.webp)</span> {#ts-229-287-5}

<span class="series-number">1081</span> “Yes, a child does not analyze what it is doing.” – Again: we are not talking about an analysis of what is happening, but only of an analysis – and this word is very misleading – of our concepts. And our concepts are more complicated than those of the child; in so far as our words have a more complicated use than his.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#287.6+288.1) <span class="fac">[287\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/287.webp),[288\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/288.webp)</span> {#ts-229-287-6--288-1}

<span class="series-number">1082</span> “But I do see it in that way, even when I don't express it.” That would mean that what I see does not change when I express it. If one asked: “Does the body have this weight only as long as it is being weighed?” – then this would mean: “Does its weight change when we put it on the scales?” And that is of course not at all what we want to ask.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#288.2) <span class="fac">[288\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/288.webp)</span> {#ts-229-288-2}

<span class="series-number">1083</span> Only through the phenomenon of the change of aspect does the aspect seem to be detached from _other ways of seeing_. It is as if, after the experience of the aspect-switch, one could say: “So there was an aspect!”


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#288.3) <span class="fac">[288\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/288.webp)</span> {#ts-229-288-3}

<span class="series-number">1084</span> If one scrapes away the _paint_ of a thing, one can say “So there was paint” – – But if the color of an object changes, can I say “It had a _color_!” – as if this had only just occurred to me? Can one say: It only became conscious to me that the thing had a color when the color changed?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#288.4) <span class="fac">[288\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/288.webp)</span> {#ts-229-288-4}

<span class="series-number">1085</span> Don’t think that it is something _strange_ that you see a picture on the wall spatially. It is – I would like to say – as ordinary as it seems. (And this I could say of many things).


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#288.5) <span class="fac">[288\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/288.webp)</span> {#ts-229-288-5}

<span class="series-number">1086</span> Imagine that the things in our environment – table, books, chairs, etc. – periodically and abruptly changed their colors; their shapes remained the same. Could one then say that we only become conscious of color as a particular component of our visual experience?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#288.6) <span class="fac">[288\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/288.webp)</span> {#ts-229-288-6}

<span class="series-number">1087</span> If I compare field and garden flowers, I can become conscious of the difference in _character_; but this does not mean that I had previously perceived the character of the flower, or that I had to perceive it in some character.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#288.7) <span class="fac">[288\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/288.webp)</span> {#ts-229-288-7}

<span class="series-number">1088</span> Must I know that I see with two eyes? Certainly not. Do I have _two_ visual impressions in ordinary seeing, so that I notice that my three-dimensional visual impression is composed of two visual images? Certainly not. – So I cannot separate three-dimensionality from seeing.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#288.8+289.1) <span class="fac">[288\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/288.webp),[289\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/289.webp)</span> {#ts-229-288-8--289-1}

<span class="series-number">1089</span> If I ask someone, “In what direction does an ‘F’ face, and in what direction does an ‘I’ face?” and he answers that an F always faces to the right, and an I to the left – then this certainly does not mean that when he looks at an F, he always has a sensation of direction. This becomes clearer if one asks _this_: “Where would you paint an eye and a nose on an F?” – But if one then said: “So it only looks in that direction for you as long as you think or say this” – is that not the same as asking: “Would you paint the nose on the F in that direction if you were to paint it?”


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#289.2) <span class="fac">[289\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/289.webp)</span> {#ts-229-289-2}

<span class="series-number">1090</span> Do I always see a face ‘_as a face_’? I have books in front of me: Do I see them all the time ‘_as books_’? Do I mean: Do I see them all the time as books, when I am not currently seeing them as something else? Or do I often, or usually, only see colors and shapes, without a particular aspect? (apparently not!) We say to someone: “If _this_ is the base, then _this_ is the apex and _this_ is the height.” Or he has to answer the question: “Which is the height of the triangle, if _this_ is the base?” But we do not insist that he _sees_ the triangle in this way. – One sometimes says “Imagine it turned around!” (or something similar), and one could also say “See it turned around,” and this remark could help; in the same way that a graphic addition to the picture could also help, suggesting this aspect.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#289.3) <span class="fac">[289\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/289.webp)</span> {#ts-229-289-3}

<span class="series-number">1091</span> Can I, for example, say: I see the armchair as an _object_, as a _unit_? Just as I say, I now see the black cross on a white background, but now the white cross on a black background? If someone asks me “What do you have in front of you?” will I of course answer “An armchair,” and thus treat it as a unit. But can one now say that I _see_ it _as a unit_? And can I look at the cross-shaped figure without _seeing_ it _in one way or another_?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#289.4) <span class="fac">[289\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/289.webp)</span> {#ts-229-289-4}

<span class="series-number">1092</span> If I ask someone “What do you see in front of you?” and he says “What I have in front of me looks _like this_,” and now he draws the cross-shaped figure – does he _have_ to have seen it in some aspect? Has he not seen it if he can only describe it graphically?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#289.5+290.1) <span class="fac">[289\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/289.webp),[290\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/290.webp)</span> {#ts-229-289-5--290-1}

<span class="series-number">1093</span> Can a child communicate to you that it sees in three dimensions? And imagine it tells you “I see everything flat” – what would that tell you? It could see everything _flat_, and through an intuition know that it is not flat, and behave accordingly!


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#290.2) <span class="fac">[290\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/290.webp)</span> {#ts-229-290-2}

<span class="series-number">1094</span> If the child takes this picture for this and that, and I conclude “So it _sees_ the picture _like this_” – what kind of conclusion am I drawing? What does this conclusion _tell_ me? One might say that I am inferring the type of sense-datum, or visual image; as if the conclusion were: “So the picture is _like this_ in its mind”; and now one would have to represent it plastically.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#290.3) <span class="fac">[290\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/290.webp)</span> {#ts-229-290-3}

<span class="series-number">1095</span> Is it _like this_: “I have always read the sign ‘🖵’ as a sigma; now someone tells me that it could also be an inverted M, and I can now see it _like that_; – therefore, I have always seen it as a sigma”? So, am I saying, I have not only seen the figure and read it _like that_, but I have also seen it _as that_!


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#290.4) <span class="fac">[290\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/290.webp)</span> {#ts-229-290-4}

<span class="series-number">1096</span> “But how could I know that I would have reacted in that way if you had asked me?” – How? There is no how. But there are signs that I am right to say it.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#290.5+291.1) <span class="fac">[290\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/290.webp),[291\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/291.webp)</span> {#ts-229-290-5--291-1}

<span class="series-number">1097</span> I want to describe what I see; I make a transparent sheet for that purpose. But now I am asked “Is _this_ in front and _this_ behind?” So I describe in words, or with a model, what I see in front, what I see behind. And now I am asked “And do you see _this_ point as the apex of the triangle?” and I have to answer that too. – But must I have an answer to that? – Suppose that it is not true that the viewing direction determines the aspect. And in _one_ case my gaze is always directed at the same point of the picture, in another case it moves regularly according to a simple rule, and in a third case it wanders randomly over the object. If we now substitute a description of the viewing direction for a description of the aspect, would it be a description to say that the viewing direction is random or undefined? And that could even be the usual case. – So the answer to the question “Did you see this point as the apex of the triangle?” can be “I cannot name a specific aspect,” or perhaps “I didn’t see it _like that_ in any case.”


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#291.2) <span class="fac">[291\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/291.webp)</span> {#ts-229-291-2}

<span class="series-number">1098</span> What, incidentally, did the hypothesis of the importance of the direction of gaze do for us? – It provided us with a picture of a certain manifold.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#291.3) <span class="fac">[291\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/291.webp)</span> {#ts-229-291-3}

<span class="series-number">1099</span> Actually, however, such a theory is the construction of a psychological model of a psychological phenomenon. And therefore a psychological model. The theory actually says: “It could be _like_ this: ‥‥” And the use of the theory is that it illustrates a concept. But it can illustrate it better or worse; more or less accurately. So the theory is, as it were, a notation for this psychological phenomenon.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#291.4) <span class="fac">[291\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/291.webp)</span> {#ts-229-291-4}

<span class="series-number">2000</span> If we therefore ‘drop the explanation’ – if we say that the _explanation_ is ultimately irrelevant to us – then a grammatical statement remains. It concerns the use of the statement “I now see a certain facial expression in the picture.”


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#291.5) <span class="fac">[291\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/291.webp)</span> {#ts-229-291-5}

<span class="series-number">2001</span> Does the topic point to anything other than itself? Oh yes! But that means: – the impression it makes on me is related to things in its environment – e.g. to the existence of our language and its intonation, which means, to the entire field of our language-games. For example, if I say: It is as if a conclusion is being drawn here, or as if something is being emphasized, or as if _this_ is an answer to what came before, – then my understanding presupposes familiarity with conclusions, emphases, answers.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#291.6) <span class="fac">[291\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/291.webp)</span> {#ts-229-291-6}

<span class="series-number">2002</span> A topic has no less of a facial expression than a face.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#291.7+292.1) <span class="fac">[291\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/291.webp),[292\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/292.webp)</span> {#ts-229-291-7--292-1}

<span class="series-number">2003</span> “Repetition is _necessary_.” In what sense is it necessary? Well, sing it, and you will see that it is only repetition that gives it its _great_ power. – Isn’t it as if there must be a template for the topic in reality, and the topic only comes close to it, only corresponds to it, if this part is repeated? Or should I say the silly thing: “It just sounds better with repetition”? And yet there _is_ no paradigm outside the topic. And yet there _is_ also a paradigm outside the topic: namely the rhythm of our language, our thinking and feeling. And the topic is again a _new_ part of our language; it is incorporated into it; we learn a new _gesture_.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#292.2) <span class="fac">[292\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/292.webp)</span> {#ts-229-292-2}

<span class="series-number">2004</span> The topic is in interaction with the language.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#292.3) <span class="fac">[292\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/292.webp)</span> {#ts-229-292-3}

<span class="series-number">2005</span> “A whole world of pain lies in these words.” How _can_ it lie in them? – It is connected with them. The words are like the acorn from which an _oak tree_ can grow. But where is the law laid down according to which the tree grows from the acorn? Well, the picture is incorporated into our thinking through experience.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#292.4) <span class="fac">[292\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/292.webp)</span> {#ts-229-292-4}

<span class="series-number">2006</span> “Where do you feel the sadness?” – In the mind. – – And if I had to indicate a place here, I would point to the stomach area. For love, I would point to the chest, and for an idea, to the head.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#292.5) <span class="fac">[292\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/292.webp)</span> {#ts-229-292-5}

<span class="series-number">2007</span> “Where do you feel the sadness?” – In the mind. – – What does that mean? – – What consequences do we draw from this localization? One is that we are _not_ talking about a physical location of sadness. But we _do_ point to our body, as if the sadness were in it. Is this because we feel a physical discomfort? I don’t know the cause. But why should I assume that it is a physical discomfort?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#292.6) <span class="fac">[292\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/292.webp)</span> {#ts-229-292-6}

<span class="series-number">2008</span> Consider the following question: Can one think of a pain, for example of the quality of rheumatic pain, but _without_ location? Can one _imagine_ it? If you start thinking about it, you will see how much you want to transform the knowledge of the location of the pain into a characteristic of the _felt_, into a characteristic of a sense-datum, the private object that stands before my mind.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#293.1) <span class="fac">[293\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/293.webp)</span> {#ts-229-293-1}

<span class="series-number">2009</span> I say that to the sorrowful person, the whole world appears gray. – But what would be before his mind would then not be sorrow, but a gray world; as if it were the cause of the sorrow.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#293.2) <span class="fac">[293\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/293.webp)</span> {#ts-229-293-2}

<span class="series-number">2010</span> To perceive something as a difference in color – and on the other hand, as a shadow with the same color. I ask, “Have you perceived the color of the table in front of you, the one you have been looking at the whole time?” He says, “Yes.” But he could have described the table as “brown,” and he did not notice that the green curtain is reflected in its shiny surface. – Did he not have the green visual impression? “Is the wall in front of you uniformly yellow?” – “Yes.” But it is partially in shadow and looks almost gray. What did the person who was looking at the wall see? Should I say, a uniformly yellow surface, which is, of course, irregularly shaded? Or: yellow and gray patches?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#293.3) <span class="fac">[293\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/293.webp)</span> {#ts-229-293-3}

<span class="series-number">2011</span> It is a remarkable fact that we are hardly ever conscious of the indistinctness of the periphery of our visual field. When people, for example, talk about a visual image, they mostly do _not_ think about it; and when one talks about a representation of the visual impression through a picture, one sees no difficulty in this. That is _very_ important.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#293.4) <span class="fac">[293\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/293.webp)</span> {#ts-229-293-4}

<span class="series-number">2012</span> “What I perceive is _**this**_ –” and then follows a form of _**description**_. One could also explain this as follows: Let us imagine a direct transmission of the experience! – But what is our criterion for the fact that the experience has actually been transmitted? “Well, he simply has the same thing that I have.” – But how does _he_ _have_ it?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#293.5) <span class="fac">[293\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/293.webp)</span> {#ts-229-293-5}

<span class="series-number">2013</span> Think of the variety of physical experiments. We measure, for example, the temperature; but only in a certain general technique is _this_ experiment a measurement of the temperature. – If we were interested in the variety of (physical) measurements, i.e., the types of measurement, then we would be interested in the variety of methods, of concepts.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#293.6+294.1) <span class="fac">[293\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/293.webp),[294\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/294.webp)</span> {#ts-229-293-6--294-1}

<span class="series-number">2014</span> How can you _look_ at sorrow? By _being_ sorrowful? By not letting anything distract you from your sorrow? So do you observe the feeling by _having_ it? And if you keep every distraction away, do you then observe _this_ state? or the other one, in which you were _before_ the observation. So are you observing your observing?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#294.2) <span class="fac">[294\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/294.webp)</span> {#ts-229-294-2}

<span class="series-number">2015</span> Suppose someone asks, “What is measured in physics?” Then one could list: lengths, times, light intensities, weights, etc. But could one not say: You learn more if you ask “How is it measured?” instead of “What is measured?” If _this_ is done, if it is measured _in this way_, then it is the temperature; if _that_ is done, it is measured _in that way_: an electric current.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#294.3) <span class="fac">[294\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/294.webp)</span> {#ts-229-294-3}

<span class="series-number">2016</span> But is sorrow not made up of all sorts of feelings? Is it not a conglomerate of feelings? Could one therefore say that it consists of the feelings A, B, C, etc. – just as granite consists of feldspar, mica, and quartz? – So do I say of someone that he is sorrowful if he has the feelings… and how do I know that he has them? Does he tell us?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#294.4) <span class="fac">[294\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/294.webp)</span> {#ts-229-294-4}

<span class="series-number">2017</span> Sorrow is, after all, a mental experience. One says that one experiences sorrow, joy, disappointment. And then these experiences seem to be actually composed and distributed over the whole body. The deep sigh of joy, the laughter, the cheering, the thoughts of happiness – is not the experience of all of that the joy? Do I know that he is joyful because he tells me that he feels his laughter, feels and hears his cheering, etc., – or because he laughs and cheers? Do I say “I am happy” because I feel all of that?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#294.5) <span class="fac">[294\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/294.webp)</span> {#ts-229-294-5}

<span class="series-number">2018</span> The words “I am happy” are a joy-behaviour.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#294.6) <span class="fac">[294\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/294.webp)</span> {#ts-229-294-6}

<span class="series-number">2019</span> And how does it come about that I – as James says – have a feeling of joy when I merely make a joyful face; a feeling of sadness when I make a sad one? That I can therefore produce these feelings by imitating their external expression? Does this show that the muscular sensations are part of sadness, or a part of the feeling of sadness?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#295.1) <span class="fac">[295\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/295.webp)</span> {#ts-229-295-1}

<span class="series-number">1120</span> Suppose someone says: “Lift your arm, and you will feel that you are lifting your arm.” Is this a statement of experience? And is it one if one says, “Make a sad face and you will feel sad”? Or did he mean: “Feel that you are making a sad face and you will feel sadness”? And is that a pleonasm?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#295.2) <span class="fac">[295\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/295.webp)</span> {#ts-229-295-2}

<span class="series-number">1121</span> Suppose I say: “Yes, it is true: when I make a friendlier face, I feel better immediately.” Is that because the feelings in the face are more pleasant? Or because it has consequences to make this face? (One says, “Keep your chin up!”)


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#295.3) <span class="fac">[295\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/295.webp)</span> {#ts-229-295-3}

<span class="series-number">1122</span> Does one say: “I feel much better now: the feeling in the facial muscles and around the corners of the mouth is good”? And why does that sound ridiculous, except perhaps if one previously had pain in these parts?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#295.4) <span class="fac">[295\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/295.webp)</span> {#ts-229-295-4}

<span class="series-number">1123</span> Does one compare my feeling in the corners of the mouth with his in the same way – and my state of mind with his? How, for example, do I compare my sensations of pressure with his? How do I _learn_ to compare them? How do I compare our kinesthetic sensations, how do I relate them to each other? And how about the feelings of sadness, joy, etc.?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#295.5) <span class="fac">[295\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/295.webp)</span> {#ts-229-295-5}

<span class="series-number">1124</span> Now, granted – although it is highly doubtful – that the muscular feeling of smiling is a component of the feeling of happiness; – but where are the other components? – Well, in the chest, in the stomach, etc.! – But do you really feel them, or do you only conclude that they _must_ be there? Are you really conscious of these localized feelings? – And if not, – why should they be there at all? Why should you _mean_ them when you say you feel happy?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#295.6) <span class="fac">[295\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/295.webp)</span> {#ts-229-295-6}

<span class="series-number">1125</span> What would have to be established through an act of _observation_, you certainly did not mean that. This is how “sadness,” “joy,” etc. are _not_ used.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#295.7+296.1) <span class="fac">[295\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/295.webp),[296\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/296.webp)</span> {#ts-229-295-7--296-1}

<span class="series-number">1126</span> Why does it sound strange: “He felt deep sadness for a moment”? Because it happens so rarely? And what if we imagined people who have this experience often? Or those who often experience, for hours, alternating for a moment of deep sadness and intense happiness.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#296.2) <span class="fac">[296\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/296.webp)</span> {#ts-229-296-2}

<span class="series-number">1127</span> “Don’t you feel the sadness _now_…” – is that the same as asking: “Are you not playing chess _now_?“ In fact, however, the question was personal and temporal, not philosophical.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#296.3) <span class="fac">[296\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/296.webp)</span> {#ts-229-296-3}

<span class="series-number">1128</span> “‘I hope… – the description of my state of mind’”: That sounds as if I were looking at my soul and describing it (as one describes a landscape). If I now say: “I keep hoping that he will come to me” – is that a hoping behavior? Isn’t it just as much a hoping behavior as the words: “I hoped then that he would come”? – Should I therefore not say that there are two kinds of present tense of “hoping”? One, as it were, an exclamation, the other a report?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#296.4) <span class="fac">[296\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/296.webp)</span> {#ts-229-296-4}

<span class="series-number">1129</span> But if I now say to someone, “I very much hope that he will come to our meeting” – he asks me: “What was that: a report, or an exclamation?” – Doesn’t he understand me if he doesn’t know that? And yet, it is one thing to say “I hope he will come” and another to say: “I do not lose hope that he will come.” Or think of this expression: “I hope and pray that he may come.”


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#296.5) <span class="fac">[296\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/296.webp)</span> {#ts-229-296-5}

<span class="series-number">1130</span> “I hope he will come” – one could say – sometimes means as much as the exclamation “He will come!”, spoken in a hopeful tone. But there need not be a perfect tense form of this exclamation. Couldn’t one imagine a language in which there is an equivalent to this exclamation of hope, but not the other forms of the verb? In which, when people do want to talk about past hope, they quote themselves; for example, saying: “I said, ‘He will certainly come!’”


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#296.6+297.1) <span class="fac">[296\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/296.webp),[297\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/297.webp)</span> {#ts-229-296-6--297-1}

<span class="series-number">1131</span> One could say: _The_ statement says something about the state of mind, from which one can infer the state of mind. (This sounds sillier than it is.) If this is _so_, then the expression of the wish “Give me this apple!!” says something about my state of mind. And is this sentence therefore a description of this state? One would not want to say that. (“Off with his head!”)


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#297.2) <span class="fac">[297\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/297.webp)</span> {#ts-229-297-2}

<span class="series-number">1132</span> Is the cry “Help!” a description of my state of mind? And is it _not_ the expression of a wish? Is it not so much so, as any other?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#297.3) <span class="fac">[297\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/297.webp)</span> {#ts-229-297-3}

<span class="series-number">1133</span> I say to myself: “I hope and I still hope, although…” – and I shake my head, as it were, at myself. This means something quite different from simply “I hope…!” (The difference in English between “I am hoping” and “I hope”.)


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#297.4) <span class="fac">[297\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/297.webp)</span> {#ts-229-297-4}

<span class="series-number">1134</span> And what does the one who observes his own hope observe? What would he _report_? Various things. “I hoped daily, … I imagined … I said to myself every day … I sighed … I walked this path every day, hoping…”


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#297.5) <span class="fac">[297\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/297.webp)</span> {#ts-229-297-5}

<span class="series-number">1135</span> The word “observe” is poorly placed here. I am trying to remember this and that.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#297.6) <span class="fac">[297\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/297.webp)</span> {#ts-229-297-6}

<span class="series-number">1136</span> The one who remembers his hope, by the way, does not remember behavior, nor necessarily thoughts. He says – he knows – that he hoped at that time.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#297.7) <span class="fac">[297\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/297.webp)</span> {#ts-229-297-7}

<span class="series-number">1137</span> The sentence “I wish to drink wine” has approximately the same sense as “Wine, please!” No one would call _this_ a description; but I can infer from it that the person saying it is eager to drink wine, that he can at any moment resort to violence if his wish is denied – and this one would call an inference to his state of mind.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#297.8) <span class="fac">[297\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/297.webp)</span> {#ts-229-297-8}

<span class="series-number">1138</span> Is “I believe…” a description of my state of mind? – Well, what _is_ such a description? Something like: “I am sad,” “I am in a good mood,” perhaps “I am in pain.”


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#297.9) <span class="fac">[297\[9\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/297.webp)</span> {#ts-229-297-9}

<span class="series-number">1139</span> It would be disastrous to regard Moore’s paradox as something that can only occur in the realm of the mental.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#298.1) <span class="fac">[298\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/298.webp)</span> {#ts-229-298-1}

<span class="series-number">1140</span> I want to say first that with the assertion “It will rain,” one expresses belief in it just as much as one expresses the wish for wine with the words “Wine, please!” One could also say: “I believe p” means approximately the same as “p”; and that in the first sentence the verb “believe” and the pronoun “I” stand, should not mislead us. It only clearly shows that the grammar of “I believe” is very different from that of “I write.” But if I say that, I do not thereby say that there cannot also be great similarities; and I do not say what _kind_ the differences are. ((real and imaginary unit.) Indeed, remember that it concerns similarities and differences of concepts, not of the phenomena.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#298.2) <span class="fac">[298\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/298.webp)</span> {#ts-229-298-2}

<span class="series-number">1141</span> One can say the following odd thing: “I believe it will rain” means something similar to “It will rain,” but “I used to believe that it would rain” does not mean something similar to “It rained back then.” But what does it mean that the first sentence has approximately the same sense as the second? Does it mean that both evoke the same thought in my mind? (the same _feeling_?) –


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#298.3) <span class="fac">[298\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/298.webp)</span> {#ts-229-298-3}

<span class="series-number">1142</span> “I want to think like _this_, and not like _that_.” And ‘_this_’ and ‘_that_’ are, however strange it may sound, not sharply separated.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#298.4) <span class="fac">[298\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/298.webp)</span> {#ts-229-298-4}

<span class="series-number">1143</span> How you use the word “God” does not show _who_ you mean, but what you mean.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#298.5) <span class="fac">[298\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/298.webp)</span> {#ts-229-298-5}

<span class="series-number">1144</span> “But it must be that ‘I used to believe’ means _that_ in the past, just as ‘I believe’ means in the present!” It must be that i means the same for ‒ 1 as 🖵 means for 1! That means nothing.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#298.6) <span class="fac">[298\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/298.webp)</span> {#ts-229-298-6}

<span class="series-number">1145</span> What does it mean that “I believe p” says approximately the same as “P”? If someone says the first and second sentences, we react in approximately the same way; if I say the first sentence and someone does not understand the words “I believe,” I would repeat the sentence in the second form, and so on. Just as I would explain “I wish that you go there” with “Go there!”


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#299.1) <span class="fac">[299\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/299.webp)</span> {#ts-229-299-1}

<span class="series-number">1146</span> Moore’s paradox can be expressed _thus_: “I believe p” says approximately the same as “P”; but “Suppose I believe p…” does not say approximately the same as “Suppose p ‥‥.” Can one understand the assumption that I wish for something before understanding the utterance of the wish? – The child first learns to express the wish, and only later to assume that it wishes for this and that.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#299.2) <span class="fac">[299\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/299.webp)</span> {#ts-229-299-2}

<span class="series-number">1147</span> “Suppose I am in pain ‥‥” – this is not an utterance of pain and therefore not pain-behaviour. The child who learns the word “pain” as an exclamation, and then begins to talk about past pain – it may one day say “If I have pain, the doctor will come.” Has the meaning of the word “pain” changed in this process of learning? Its use has changed; but one must be very careful not to interpret this change as a change of the object, to which the word now corresponds.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#299.3) <span class="fac">[299\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/299.webp)</span> {#ts-229-299-3}

<span class="series-number">1148</span> Imagine “I believe ‥‥” represented by a painting. How could I imagine that? The picture would show me and some image in my mind. It does not matter what symbolism it uses. The image of _what_ I believe, e.g., that it is raining – will be in it. My mind will perhaps grasp this image, hold onto it, and so on. – And now let us assume that this image is used as the assertion “It is raining.” Well, there is nothing strange about that. Should I say that there is now much in the picture that is _superfluous_? I would not want to say that.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#299.4) <span class="fac">[299\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/299.webp)</span> {#ts-229-299-4}

<span class="series-number">1149</span> “In essence, with these words I describe my own state of mind – but this description is here indirectly an assertion of the believed fact itself.” – – Just as I might, under certain circumstances, describe a photograph in order to describe what the photograph is a recording of.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#300.1) <span class="fac">[300\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/300.webp)</span> {#ts-229-300-1}

<span class="series-number">1150</span> But if this analogy held, I would still have to be able to say that this photograph (the impression on my mind) is reliable. I would therefore have to be able to say: “I believe that it will rain, and my belief is reliable, so I rely on it.” So, as if my belief were a kind of sense impression.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#300.2) <span class="fac">[300\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/300.webp)</span> {#ts-229-300-2}

<span class="series-number">1151</span> Are you saying: “I believe it, and since I am reliable, it will also be so”? That would be as if one said: “I believe it – therefore I believe it.”


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#300.3) <span class="fac">[300\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/300.webp)</span> {#ts-229-300-3}

<span class="series-number">1152</span> Just as one can, through the same activity, measure the length of the table, check the standard, or test the person measuring for accuracy in measuring, so a sentence can serve me to inform myself about its content, or about the character, or the state of mind of the one making the assertion.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#300.4) <span class="fac">[300\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/300.webp)</span> {#ts-229-300-4}

<span class="series-number">1153</span> One could say: “He is coming, but I still cannot believe it!” – “He is coming! I cannot believe it!”


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#300.5) <span class="fac">[300\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/300.webp)</span> {#ts-229-300-5}

<span class="series-number">1154</span> Imagine an announcer at a station who, according to schedule, announces an arriving train, but – perhaps without reason – is convinced that it will not arrive. He could announce: “Train No. ‥‥ will arrive at ‥‥․ o’clock. I personally do not believe it.”


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#300.6) <span class="fac">[300\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/300.webp)</span> {#ts-229-300-6}

<span class="series-number">1155</span> What if a soldier made military reports that, based on the observations, would be justified; but he added that he believes they are incorrect? – Let us not ask what is going on in the mind of the one who speaks like this, but rather whether others can make something of this report, and what.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#300.7+301.1) <span class="fac">[300\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/300.webp),[301\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/301.webp)</span> {#ts-229-300-7--301-1}

<span class="series-number">1156</span> The report is a language-game with these words. It would create confusion if we said: the words of the report, the reported sentence, have a certain meaning, and that the reporting, the ‘assertion,’ adds something to it. As if the sentence, spoken by a gramophone, belonged to pure logic, as if it had the purely logical meaning here, as if we had the object before us that the logician can take into his hand and examine – while the asserted, reported sentence is the thing _in commerce_. As one can say: the botanist considers a rose _as a plant_, not as an adornment of a dress or room, or as a delicate gesture. The sentence, I want to say, has no meaning outside of the language-game. This is connected with the fact that it is not a kind of _name_. So that one could say: “‘I believe…’ – that is _so_” whereby one (in oneself, roughly) points to what gives the sentence its meaning.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#301.2) <span class="fac">[301\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/301.webp)</span> {#ts-229-301-2}

<span class="series-number">1157</span> Is it a tautology to report: “The cavalry will arrive immediately; and I believe it”?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#301.3) <span class="fac">[301\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/301.webp)</span> {#ts-229-301-3}

<span class="series-number">1158</span> The paradox is this: the _assumption_ can be expressed as: “Suppose, this is happening _in_ me and _that_ outside of me” – – but the _assertion_, that this is happening _in_ me, _says_: it is happening outside of me. In the _assumption_, the two sentences about the inside and the outside are completely independent; in the assertion, however, they are not.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#301.4) <span class="fac">[301\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/301.webp)</span> {#ts-229-301-4}

<span class="series-number">1159</span> Is this in the essence of the concept “believe”? Certainly.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#301.5) <span class="fac">[301\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/301.webp)</span> {#ts-229-301-5}

<span class="series-number">1160</span> Imagine someone saying, “I wish,” – but does not want his wish to be fulfilled. – (Lessing: “If God had in his right hand ‥‥”) Can one therefore ask God to grant the wish, and not to fulfill it?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#301.6) <span class="fac">[301\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/301.webp)</span> {#ts-229-301-6}

<span class="series-number">1161</span> It seems as though the assertion “I believe…” is not the assertion of what the assumption “I believe” assumes!


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#301.7) <span class="fac">[301\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/301.webp)</span> {#ts-229-301-7}

<span class="series-number">1162</span> Do not take it for granted, but as something very remarkable, that the verbs “believe,” “hope,” “wish,” “intend,” etc., all have the grammatical forms that “eat,” “talk,” “cut” also have.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#301.8+302.1) <span class="fac">[301\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/301.webp),[302\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/302.webp)</span> {#ts-229-301-8--302-1}

<span class="series-number">1163</span> Suppose I were the hybrid being that could utter, “I do not believe it is raining; and it is raining.” – But what is the use of these words now? What use do I imagine them having? “He is coming. I personally do not believe it, but do not let that deter you.” – “He is coming, you can rely on it. _I_ do not believe it; but do not let that deter you.” This sounds as if two people were speaking from within me; or as if one instance within me communicated to the other that he is coming, and this instance wishes the other to act accordingly, – while another instance, in a certain sense, announces my own behaviour. It is as if one said: “I know that this way of acting is wrong, but I know that I will act this way.” “He is coming, but I do not believe it” can therefore occur in a language-game. Or rather: “One can imagine a language-game in which these words would not seem absurd to us.”


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#302.2) <span class="fac">[302\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/302.webp)</span> {#ts-229-302-2}

<span class="series-number">1164</span> A voltmeter, instead of indicating the voltage by means of a pointer and dial, could express it by means of a gramophone record. The instrument says, for example, when one presses a button (it asks), “The voltage is ‥‥” Could it also make sense for the voltmeter to say: “I believe the voltage is ‥‥”? – One can certainly imagine such a case. Should I then say that the instrument is saying something about itself, – or about the voltage? Should I say that the instrument always says something about itself? And if it, for example, can repeat a higher reading of the voltage: has it _believed_ that the voltage was ‥‥?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#302.3) <span class="fac">[302\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/302.webp)</span> {#ts-229-302-3}

<span class="series-number">1165</span> Or let us put it this way: Should I say that a voltmeter indicates something about itself, or about the voltage? Can I not say both? Namely, each under different circumstances?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#303.1) <span class="fac">[303\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/303.webp)</span> {#ts-229-303-1}

<span class="series-number">1166</span> Do “Help!” and “I need help” have different sense; is it only a crudeness of our perception that we regard them as synonymous? Does it always mean saying something: “Strictly speaking, what I meant was not ‘Help!’, but ‘I want help.’” The worst enemy of our understanding is here the idea, the image, of a ‘sense’ of what we are saying in our mind.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#303.2) <span class="fac">[303\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/303.webp)</span> {#ts-229-303-2}

<span class="series-number">1167</span> The assertion “He will come” does not refer to the person making the assertion. But neither does it refer to the words of the assertion, while “‘he will come’ is a true sentence” refers to the words and has the same sense as the sentence that does not.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#303.3) <span class="fac">[303\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/303.webp)</span> {#ts-229-303-3}

<span class="series-number">1168</span> Could one speak of the sense of the words “that he will come”? For these words are, in fact, the Fregean ‘assumption’. Now, could I not explain to someone what this expression means? Yes, by explaining, or showing, how it is used.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#303.4) <span class="fac">[303\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/303.webp)</span> {#ts-229-303-4}

<span class="series-number">1169</span> The difficulty becomes insurmountable if you think that the sentence “I believe ‥‥” says something about the state of my mind. If this were so, then one would have to be able to reproduce the Moorean paradox if one said something about the state of the brain, for example, instead of about the state of one's own mind. But the point is precisely that no assertion about the state of my brain (or anyone else) is equivalent to the assertion that I believe – “He will come,” for example.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#303.5+304.1) <span class="fac">[303\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/303.webp),[304\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/304.webp)</span> {#ts-229-303-5--304-1}

<span class="series-number">1170</span> But let us nevertheless take the assertion “He believes p” as an assertion about his _state_, from which it certainly emerges how he will behave under given circumstances! Is there then no _first person present_ in such an assertion? Can I not then say of myself that I am now in a state in which the one or the other linguistic, and other, reaction is likely? It is certainly similar when I say, “I am very irritable now.” Similarly, I could also say “I now believe any bad news very easily.”


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#304.2) <span class="fac">[304\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/304.webp)</span> {#ts-229-304-2}

<span class="series-number">1171</span> Would a sentence, which asserts that I – or my brain – am now in such a state that I would answer the question “Will he come?” with “Yes,” and exhibit the one and the other reactions, be equivalent to the assertion “He will come”?

One might ask here: “How do you imagine that I am informed about this state of mine? – Through experience, perhaps? Do I want to predict, from experience, that I will always answer such a question in this way, etc.?” If this is the case, and if I make the statement “I believe that he will come” and add “and he will not come,” then this is only a contradiction in the same way as, for example, this is a contradiction: “I cannot pronounce a four-syllable word,” or this: “I cannot say a single German sentence.” If the latter is a kind of contradiction, then it is not the assumption: “Assume that I could not say a single German sentence.”


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#304.3) <span class="fac">[304\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/304.webp)</span> {#ts-229-304-3}

<span class="series-number">1172</span> The fact that he believes this and that emerges for us from the observation of his person, but he does not make the statement “I believe ‥‥” on the basis of self-observation. And therefore, “I believe p” can be equivalent to the assertion of “p.” Therefore also the question “Is this so?” is equivalent to the sentence “I would like to know whether this is so.”


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#304.4+305.1) <span class="fac">[304\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/304.webp),[305\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/305.webp)</span> {#ts-229-304-4--305-1}

<span class="series-number">1173</span> “This face has a very specific character” actually means: one could say a lot about it. When does one say this? What justifies one in doing so? Is it a certain experience? Does one already know what one will say; has one already imagined it in silence? Is the situation not similar to this: “Now I know what to do!”


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#305.2) <span class="fac">[305\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/305.webp)</span> {#ts-229-305-2}

<span class="series-number">1174</span> We all know the process of the momentary change of aspect; but how, if one were to ask: Has A the aspect a continuously before him – if, namely, no change of aspect has occurred? Can the _aspect_ not, so to speak, become _fresher_ or _more indefinite_? – And how strange that I _ask_ this!


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#305.3) <span class="fac">[305\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/305.webp)</span> {#ts-229-305-3}

<span class="series-number">1175</span> There is such a thing as a fleeting aspect. Just as one can play something with more or less intense expression. With stronger emphasis on rhythm and structure, or less strong emphasis.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#305.4) <span class="fac">[305\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/305.webp)</span> {#ts-229-305-4}

<span class="series-number">1176</span> To see _that_ as a variation of _this_. So there is the moment when, upon seeing A, I _think_ of B, where this seeing is, so to speak, acute, and then the time when it is chronic.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#305.5) <span class="fac">[305\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/305.webp)</span> {#ts-229-305-5}

<span class="series-number">1177</span> It is difficult not to explain the psychological phenomenon, but to _accept_ it.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#305.6) <span class="fac">[305\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/305.webp)</span> {#ts-229-305-6}

<span class="series-number">1178</span> “F” as a variation of different figures. If I think that the paradigm, as a variation of which I see the object, is somehow present in my mind while seeing it, then it could be present more clearly or less clearly, and it could also disappear completely.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#306.1) <span class="fac">[306\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/306.webp)</span> {#ts-229-306-1}

<span class="series-number">1179</span> Imagine two people: one learned “F” like this 🖵 in his youth – the other, as we do 🖵. If the two of them now read the word “figure,” must I say that I have reason to say that they each see the “F” differently? Apparently not. And could it not also be that one of them, when he hears how the other person learned to write and read this letter, says: “I have _never_ seen it like that, but always _like this_”? And furthermore, there will probably be situations in which I will explain what one of these people does or says in this way: “He is, namely, considering this letter as a variation of ‥‥”


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#306.2) <span class="fac">[306\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/306.webp)</span> {#ts-229-306-2}

<span class="series-number">1180</span> It is certain that one can say: “I have never seen that before.” Here, “never” is undoubtedly true. – But if you say “I have _always_ seen it like this,” then this “always” is not equally certain. And there is, of course, nothing strange about this if one says “understood” instead of “seen.”


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#306.3) <span class="fac">[306\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/306.webp)</span> {#ts-229-306-3}

<span class="series-number">1181</span> Suppose you knew that the sign 🖵 is a combination of a 🖵 with a 🖵. – – This reminds one of the dream phenomenon, which one describes in a dream narrative with the words: “and I knew that …”. And it also has similarity with what one calls “hallucination”.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#307.1) <span class="fac">[307\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/307.webp)</span> {#ts-229-307-1}

<span class="series-number">1182</span> It is as if a paradigm, a template, is present in my mind when I see the sign. But what kind of template? What does it look like? But not exactly like the sign itself! – So like the sign, seen in that way? – But how seen? How should one note the aspect? Well, how do we note it; how do we communicate about it? I might say: “The sign, as I see it, looks to the right.” I could even speak of a kind of visual focal point, – say: The focal point of the sign 🖵 is located _here_. Can I explain what I mean by that? No. – But I can compare this reaction of mine with the reactions of others.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#307.2) <span class="fac">[307\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/307.webp)</span> {#ts-229-307-2}

<span class="series-number">1183</span> Am I always conscious of the blurriness of the edges of my field of vision? Should I say: “Almost never”, or “Never”?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#307.3) <span class="fac">[307\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/307.webp)</span> {#ts-229-307-3}

<span class="series-number">1184</span> In another space of thought – one might say – the thing looks different.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#307.4) <span class="fac">[307\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/307.webp)</span> {#ts-229-307-4}

<span class="series-number">1185</span> One could imagine a variation on a theme in music, which, if phrased slightly differently, can be understood as a completely different type of variation of the theme. (There are such ambiguities in rhythm.) Yes, what I mean can probably always be found, when a repetition makes the theme appear in a completely different light.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#308.1) <span class="fac">[308\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/308.webp)</span> {#ts-229-308-1}

<span class="series-number">1186</span> No aspect that is not (also) perception.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#308.2) <span class="fac">[308\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/308.webp)</span> {#ts-229-308-2}

<span class="series-number">1187</span> Suppose someone said to me: “Something has now changed in the _picture_ – I cannot express it any other way – although the form is the same as before. I can only say: before it was a kind of 🖵, now it is a kind of 🖵”. If he said _that_, could one not still doubt that he has always, continuously, seen the figure in that way and has not only never understood it differently?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#308.3) <span class="fac">[308\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/308.webp)</span> {#ts-229-308-3}

<span class="series-number">1188</span> Imagine the child, when it has learned the letter “R”, says to us: “I always see it as an ‘R’”. What could this communicate to us? – Yes, even if it said to us, “I always see it as a ‘P’ with a slanted support,” that would only tell us: that is how the child understands it, that is how it explains the letter to itself, and so on. Only when it speaks of a change of aspect would we say, now it is that phenomenon…


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#308.4) <span class="fac">[308\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/308.webp)</span> {#ts-229-308-4}

<span class="series-number">1189</span> If someone says “I always see it _like this_”, then he must specify the “_like this_”. Suppose he does this by following the lines of the figure in a certain sequence, or in a certain rhythm. That would be _similar_ to saying: “I always follow the figure with my eyes _like this_”. And in that case, of course, it could be that his memory deceives him.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#309.1) <span class="fac">[309\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/309.webp)</span> {#ts-229-309-1}

<span class="series-number">1190</span> If he says “I see (now) the figure _like this_” and follows it in a certain way, then that must be not only a description, but, as it were, the seeing itself. But if he says “I _have_ always seen it like that”, that means that he has never seen it _differently_, and in that he may be mistaken.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#309.2) <span class="fac">[309\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/309.webp)</span> {#ts-229-309-2}

<span class="series-number">1191</span> No, the paradigm was not constantly present in my mind – – but when I describe the change of aspect, then I describe it by means of paradigms.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#309.3) <span class="fac">[309\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/309.webp)</span> {#ts-229-309-3}

<span class="series-number">1192</span> “I have always seen it _like this_” – with this one actually wants to say: “I have always understood it _like this_, and _this_ change of aspect has never taken place.”


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#309.4) <span class="fac">[309\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/309.webp)</span> {#ts-229-309-4}

<span class="series-number">1193</span> “I have never seen it _like this_, but always _like this_.” But from that _alone_, there is still no sentence. The field is still missing.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#309.5+310.1) <span class="fac">[309\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/309.webp),[310\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/310.webp)</span> {#ts-229-309-5--310-1}

<span class="series-number">1194</span> “I have always seen it with _this_ visual image.” But you must also say, with _which_ one. And as soon as you _add_ _that_, it is no longer as if you had always done so. “I have always seen this letter with a grumpy visual image.” One can ask: “Are you sure that it was _always_ so?” I.e.: Was the grumpiness always striking to you?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#310.2) <span class="fac">[310\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/310.webp)</span> {#ts-229-310-2}

<span class="series-number">1195</span> And what about the ‘being struck’? Does this happen in a moment, or does it last?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#310.3) <span class="fac">[310\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/310.webp)</span> {#ts-229-310-3}

<span class="series-number">1196</span> “When I look at it, I always see the visual image of his father.” Always? – But not just for _moments_! This aspect can last.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#310.4) <span class="fac">[310\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/310.webp)</span> {#ts-229-310-4}

<span class="series-number">1197</span> Imagine someone said: “I now always see it in _this_ context.” –


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#310.5) <span class="fac">[310\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/310.webp)</span> {#ts-229-310-5}

<span class="series-number">1198</span> Absolute and relative pitch: Here is something similar: I hear the transition from one tone to another. But after a short time, I can no longer recognize a tone as being higher or lower than those two. And it should also make no sense to speak of such a “recognition”; if there is no criterion for _correct_ recognition.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#310.6) <span class="fac">[310\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/310.webp)</span> {#ts-229-310-6}

<span class="series-number">1199</span> It is almost as if ‘_seeing_ the sign in _this_ context’ were an echo of a thought.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#310.7+311.1) <span class="fac">[310\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/310.webp),[311\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/311.webp)</span> {#ts-229-310-7--311-1}

<span class="series-number">1200</span> To say of a real or painted visual image “I have always seen it as a visual image” would be odd; but not: “It was always a visual image for me, and I never saw it _as_ _something else_.”


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#311.2) <span class="fac">[311\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/311.webp)</span> {#ts-229-311-2}

<span class="series-number">1201</span> If I, for example, once see the 🖵 as a T with an added stroke, then it is as if the _grouping_ has changed. But if I am asked: “So, before, you always saw this figure with the grouping of an F?” then I could not say that it was so.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#311.3) <span class="fac">[311\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/311.webp)</span> {#ts-229-311-3}

<span class="series-number">1202</span> If someone says: “I am talking about a visual phenomenon in which the visual image, namely its organization, actually changes, although the shapes and colors remain the same,” then I can answer him: “I know what you are talking about; I _also want to say_ what you are saying.” – So I am not saying: “Yes, the phenomenon that we are both talking about is really a change in the organization ‥‥”, but “Yes, this talk of the change in the organization, etc., is the manifestation of the experience that I also mean.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#311.4) <span class="fac">[311\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/311.webp)</span> {#ts-229-311-4}

<span class="series-number">1203</span> “The organization of the visual image changes.” – “Yes, I also want to say that.” This is analogous to when someone says, “Everything around me seems unreal” – and another replies: “Yes, I know this phenomenon. I would also like to express it in the same way.”


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#311.5+312.1) <span class="fac">[311\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/311.webp),[312\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/312.webp)</span> {#ts-229-311-5--312-1}

<span class="series-number">1204</span> “The organization of the visual image changes” does not have the same kind of application as: “The organization of this association changes.” _Here_ I can describe _how_ it is when the organization of our association changes.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#312.2) <span class="fac">[312\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/312.webp)</span> {#ts-229-312-2}

<span class="series-number">1205</span> “It never occurred to me that one could see the figure _like_ that”: does that mean that it did occur to me, or that I knew that one could see it _like_ that, as I have always seen it?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#312.3) <span class="fac">[312\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/312.webp)</span> {#ts-229-312-3}

<span class="series-number">1206</span> I hear a tone – so am I not hearing how loud it is? – Is it correct to say: if I hear the tone, I must be conscious of its degree of loudness? – It is different when its strength changes.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#312.4) <span class="fac">[312\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/312.webp)</span> {#ts-229-312-4}

<span class="series-number">1207</span> At first glance, it would seem _so_: Someone realizes that one can also see an F as a T with an appendage; he says “Now I see it as a T, etc., now again as an F.” From this, it seems to follow that he sees it the second time as he always saw it before his discovery. – That if it made sense to say, “Now I see it again as an F,” it would also have made sense _before_ the change of aspect to say “I always see the letter F as an F.”


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#312.5+313.1) <span class="fac">[312\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/312.webp),[313\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/313.webp)</span> {#ts-229-312-5--313-1}
