<span class="series-number">1352</span> But is the past tense of the question then justified? Yes, because I oppose a change of attitude to a constancy. I really don’t just want to know what he means now, but also what he meant. – One could, for example, ask “What do you mean? and have you changed your mind?” If the answer to this question is “No”, then he also meant it earlier, according to the explanation. I want to say: the criteria for what happened in the past are different here than, for example, for the appearance of a picture.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#347.4+348.1) <span class="fac">[347\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/347.webp),[348\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/348.webp)</span> {#ts-229-347-4--348-1}

<span class="series-number">1353</span> How am I to describe this psychological phenomenon? That one can, on command, mean something in a particular way by a word? that one imagines oneself to mean it in one way or another? Should I say that the word “mean” is used here in a different sense; that one should actually use a different word? Should I propose such a word? – Or is this precisely the phenomenon that we are using the word “mean” here, which we have learned for another purpose?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#348.2) <span class="fac">[348\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/348.webp)</span> {#ts-229-348-2}

<span class="series-number">1354</span> Is it a very primitive language-game in which one says: “This word made me think of ‥‥”?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#348.3) <span class="fac">[348\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/348.webp)</span> {#ts-229-348-3}

<span class="series-number">1355</span> Instead of “I meant _that_ with the word”, one could also say “The word stood for ‥‥”. And how could the word, when I pronounced it, stand for this, and not for that?! And yet, it has precisely _this_ appearance. Is this, then, a kind of optical illusion? (So, as if the word _reflected_ the object that the explanation assigns to it.) And if this is an optical illusion, what do people lose who are not familiar with this illusion? They should lose very little.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#348.4) <span class="fac">[348\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/348.webp)</span> {#ts-229-348-4}

<span class="series-number">1356</span> The particular experience of meaning is characterized by the fact that we react with an explanation and the past tense: just as if we were explaining the meaning of a word for practical purposes.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#348.5) <span class="fac">[348\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/348.webp)</span> {#ts-229-348-5}

<span class="series-number">1357</span> The intention may change, and at the same time also a content of experience, but the intention was not an experience.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#348.6+349.1) <span class="fac">[348\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/348.webp),[349\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/349.webp)</span> {#ts-229-348-6--349-1}

<span class="series-number">1358</span> One of the principles of observation must be that I do not disturb the phenomenon I am observing through my observation. That is, my observation must be useful, applicable to the cases in which no observation is made.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#349.2) <span class="fac">[349\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/349.webp)</span> {#ts-229-349-2}

<span class="series-number">1359</span> Does this sudden realization, “Now I know it!” really correspond to a particular experience? No. – Imagine someone who always exclaims, “Now I’ve got it!” when they haven’t grasped anything; – what should we say about him? What experience did he have? It is not the particular ‘content of the experience’ during the realization that gives it its special interest, and when one says that they understood everything at that moment, that is not a description of the content of an experience. – But why not? – I want to distinguish between a statement that “I saw the formula before me at that moment” and one such as “I grasped the method at that moment.” But not as if I wanted to say – “because one cannot grasp a method in a single moment.” One can, in fact, and it happens _very_ often. – I want to say: “‘Now I understand’ is a _signal_, not a description.” And _what_ is achieved by me saying this? Well, attention is thereby directed to the origin of such a signal; the question “How does one learn the words ‘Now I understand’ and how, for example, the description of an image?” comes to the fore. For the word “signal” points to a process that is being signaled.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#349.3+350.1) <span class="fac">[349\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/349.webp),[350\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/350.webp)</span> {#ts-229-349-3--350-1}

<span class="series-number">1360</span> It is indeed its undeniable nature that favors the image: it would describe something that only we see and the other does not see, something that is therefore close to us and always accessible, but hidden from the other, something that lies _within_ us and we become aware of by looking within ourselves. And psychology is now the doctrine of this inner world.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#350.2) <span class="fac">[350\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/350.webp)</span> {#ts-229-350-2}

<span class="series-number">1361</span> If I want to say that our ‘utterances’, with which psychology deals, are by no means all descriptions of the content of experiences, then I must say that what one calls descriptions of the content of experiences is only a small group of those ‘unquestionable’ utterances. But what grammatical features characterize this group?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#350.3) <span class="fac">[350\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/350.webp)</span> {#ts-229-350-3}

<span class="series-number">1362</span> The content of an experience is what a picture can reproduce; a picture in its subjective meaning, when it says: “_This_ is what I see, – whatever the object may be that produces this impression.” For the content of the experience is the private _object_. – But how can pain then form such a content? – Rather, the sensation of temperature. And the sense of hearing is even more closely related to sight; – but already quite different.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#350.4+351.1) <span class="fac">[350\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/350.webp),[351\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/351.webp)</span> {#ts-229-350-4--351-1}

<span class="series-number">1363</span> It almost seems as if pain has a body, as if it were a thing, a body with shape and color. Why? Does it have the shape of the painful part of the body? One might say, for example, “I could _describe_ the pain if I only had the necessary words and elementary meanings for it.” One feels that one only lacks the necessary nomenclature. (James.) As if one could even paint the sensation, if only the other understood this mode of expression. – And one can indeed describe the pain spatially and temporally.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#351.2) <span class="fac">[351\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/351.webp)</span> {#ts-229-351-2}

<span class="series-number">1364</span> If the expression of pain were only a scream and its intensity depended only on the available breath, but not on the injury, – would we then also be inclined to regard the pain as something observed?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#351.3) <span class="fac">[351\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/351.webp)</span> {#ts-229-351-3}

<span class="series-number">1365</span> Why do you think that the pain of the _other_ is similar to his sensation of sight? – Or like this: Why do we group sight, hearing, and tactile sensation together? Because we ‘learn about the external world’ through them? Pain could be understood as a kind of tactile sensation.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#351.4) <span class="fac">[351\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/351.webp)</span> {#ts-229-351-4}

<span class="series-number">1366</span> But what about my idea that we do not really judge the positions and movements of our limbs according to the feelings that these movements give us? And why should we judge the surface texture of objects in this way, if one cannot say the same of our movements? – What is the criterion for the fact that our _feeling_ teaches us this?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#351.5) <span class="fac">[351\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/351.webp)</span> {#ts-229-351-5}

<span class="series-number">1367</span> How does one determine whether fatigue (for example) is an unclearly localized bodily feeling?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#352.1) <span class="fac">[352\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/352.webp)</span> {#ts-229-352-1}

<span class="series-number">1368</span> One might say that “I believe…” cannot _actually_ be the present tense of “I believed.” Or: one would have to be able to use a verb in such a way that its past tense has the sense of “I believed,” while its present tense has a different sense than our “I believe.” Or also like this: there would have to be a verb whose third person in the present tense has the sense of “he believes,” but whose first person has a different sense than “I believe.” But if this is the case, should there also be a verb whose first person says “I believe,” but whose third person does not mean what we mean by “he believes”? So the third person would also have to be beyond dispute?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#352.2) <span class="fac">[352\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/352.webp)</span> {#ts-229-352-2}

<span class="series-number">1369</span> What if someone said: “I _know_ that it will not rain, but I _believe_ that it will rain”?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#352.3) <span class="fac">[352\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/352.webp)</span> {#ts-229-352-3}

<span class="series-number">1370</span> What do sensory experiences have in common? The answer that they teach us about the external world is both false and correct. It is correct insofar as it is supposed to indicate a _logical_ criterion.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#352.4+353.1) <span class="fac">[352\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/352.webp),[353\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/353.webp)</span> {#ts-229-352-4--353-1}

<span class="series-number">1371</span> Could one conceive of an “I have lied” that I derive from observing my behaviour? Only if the other person also cannot make the _confession_ “I have lied.” Does “I have not lied” describe an experience, or “I made this statement in good faith”? – You must remember that I do not derive his good faith only from this or that behaviour, but also assume his word for it, which he does _not_ base on self-observation.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#353.2) <span class="fac">[353\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/353.webp)</span> {#ts-229-353-2}

<span class="series-number">1372</span> How is it that I cannot infer from my statement “It will rain” that I believe this? Can I not draw any interesting conclusions from the fact that I said this? If the other person says it, then I conclude, for example, that he will take an umbrella. Why not in my own case? Of course, the temptation here is to say: in my own case, I do _not_ need to draw this conclusion from my words, because I can draw it from my state of mind, from my belief itself.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#353.3) <span class="fac">[353\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/353.webp)</span> {#ts-229-353-3}

<span class="series-number">1373</span> Why do I never infer from my words about my probable actions? For the same reason that I do not infer from my facial expression about my probable behaviour. – Because the interesting thing is not that I do not infer my emotional state from my expression of emotion, but that I also do not infer my later behaviour from that expression, as others do who observe me.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#353.4) <span class="fac">[353\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/353.webp)</span> {#ts-229-353-4}

<span class="series-number">1374</span> When one philosophizes, one often makes the wrong, inappropriate _gesture_ with a word.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#354.1) <span class="fac">[354\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/354.webp)</span> {#ts-229-354-1}

<span class="series-number">1375</span> If someone meets me on the street and asks, “Where are you going?” and I answer, “I don’t know,” he assumes that I have no specific _intention_; not that I do not know whether I will be able to carry out my intention. (Hebel.)


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#354.2) <span class="fac">[354\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/354.webp)</span> {#ts-229-354-2}

<span class="series-number">1376</span> My super-ego could say of my ego: “It is raining, and the ego believes it,” and could continue: “So it will probably take an umbrella.” And how does the game continue?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#354.3) <span class="fac">[354\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/354.webp)</span> {#ts-229-354-3}

<span class="series-number">1377</span> Also consider the statement: “I will probably ‥‥” – where what follows is an arbitrary, not an involuntary action.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#354.4) <span class="fac">[354\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/354.webp)</span> {#ts-229-354-4}

<span class="series-number">1378</span> One says, for example: “One _feels_ conviction; one does not infer it from one’s own words, or their tone.” But what does it mean to _feel_ conviction? It is true: one does not infer one’s own conviction from one’s own words; or from the actions that spring from it.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#354.5+355.1) <span class="fac">[354\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/354.webp),[355\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/355.webp)</span> {#ts-229-354-5--355-1}

<span class="series-number">1379</span> To the question “Why don’t I infer my likely actions from my words?” one could say that it is as if, as an official in a ministry, I do not infer the likely decisions of the ministry from its official _utterances_, because I am familiar with the origin, the genesis of these utterances and the decisions. – This case could be compared to the one where I have internal monologues, perhaps even in writing, which lead to my spoken utterances in conversation with others; and now I say: I will not infer my future behaviour from these utterances, but from the much more reliable documents of my inner life.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#355.2) <span class="fac">[355\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/355.webp)</span> {#ts-229-355-2}

<span class="series-number">1380</span> If I am angry, I don’t need to learn about it from my behaviour. – But do I infer a likely action from my anger? One could, I think, also say: I do not observe my _actions_.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#355.3) <span class="fac">[355\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/355.webp)</span> {#ts-229-355-3}

<span class="series-number">1381</span> If I tell someone “I know that you will act that way,” then the best way to make this prediction come true is to persuade the other person to perform the action.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#355.4) <span class="fac">[355\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/355.webp)</span> {#ts-229-355-4}

<span class="series-number">1382</span> If I tell someone “You will now raise your hand,” then this prediction can be a sufficient reason for it not to come true; unless it is a command and the other person respects it.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#355.5+356.1) <span class="fac">[355\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/355.webp),[356\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/356.webp)</span> {#ts-229-355-5--356-1}

<span class="series-number">1383</span> “It is raining and I believe that it is raining.” – If I turn to the weather, I say that it is raining; then, if I turn to myself, that I believe this. – But what do I do when I turn to myself, what do I observe? Imagine, I say “It is raining and I believe that it will soon stop” – do I turn to myself in the second part of the statement? – Yes, if I want to find out whether _he_ believes it, then I must turn to him, observe him. And if I wanted to ascertain what I believe through observation, I would have to observe my _actions_, just as in the other case I observe his. Why then do I not observe them? Are they not interesting to me? They apparently are _not_. I almost never ask another person, who has observed me, whether he has the impression that I believe this and that: namely, in order to be able to infer my actions in the future in this way. Why shouldn’t a really good observer be able to predict my behaviour more accurately from my words and actions than I can myself? But perhaps I will only act as he predicts if he does not predict it to me.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#356.2) <span class="fac">[356\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/356.webp)</span> {#ts-229-356-2}

<span class="series-number">1384</span> If I say “I remember, I believed ‥‥”, then do not ask “To what fact, to what event did he refer?” (this has already been established) – but ask: “What is the purpose of this statement, how is it used?”


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#356.3) <span class="fac">[356\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/356.webp)</span> {#ts-229-356-3}

<span class="series-number">1385</span> The sense of sight, the sense of hearing, the sense of touch can fail, so that I am blind, deaf, etc.; but what would be the equivalent in the area of intention? And how would a person without imagination behave? Or one who cannot be sad and cheerful?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#357.1) <span class="fac">[357\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/357.webp)</span> {#ts-229-357-1}

<span class="series-number">1386</span> “Hope is directed towards the future” – but is there a feeling that is identical with the feeling of hope, but directed towards the present or the past? As it were, the same mental movement, but with a different object? Ask yourself: what would be regarded as the criterion for the sameness of mental movements here? Connected with this: “Is the exclamation ‘Now I can do it’ a special, specific exclamation?”


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#357.2) <span class="fac">[357\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/357.webp)</span> {#ts-229-357-2}

<span class="series-number">1387</span> Even if I grant that I know more about my own belief than about that of the other person, I must still say that I can know _precisely that_ about myself, which I know about the other person, although much more besides. – So, I should, even if it were superfluous, be able to apply a verb to myself in the same way as the word “believe” to the other person. What prevents me from doing so?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#357.3) <span class="fac">[357\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/357.webp)</span> {#ts-229-357-3}

<span class="series-number">1388</span> The concept of the world of consciousness. We populate a space with sensations.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#357.4) <span class="fac">[357\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/357.webp)</span> {#ts-229-357-4}

<span class="series-number">1389</span> “The ideal clock would simply always show the time ‘now’.” This is also connected with language, which only describes my impressions in the present moment. Related to the original statement, which is only an unarticulated sound. (Driesch.) The ideal name, which is the word “this.”


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#358.1) <span class="fac">[358\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/358.webp)</span> {#ts-229-358-1}

<span class="series-number">1390</span> I would like to talk about a family tree of psychological concepts. (Is there a similarity here with a family tree of different number concepts?)


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#358.2) <span class="fac">[358\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/358.webp)</span> {#ts-229-358-2}

<span class="series-number">1391</span> The difficulty of giving up every theory: One must consider this and that, which seems so incomplete, as something complete.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#358.3) <span class="fac">[358\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/358.webp)</span> {#ts-229-358-3}

<span class="series-number">1392</span> Fear borrows the images of dread. “I have the feeling of impending doom.”


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#358.4) <span class="fac">[358\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/358.webp)</span> {#ts-229-358-4}

<span class="series-number">1393</span> But what is the content, the content of consciousness of fear? The question is wrongly formulated.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#358.5) <span class="fac">[358\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/358.webp)</span> {#ts-229-358-5}

<span class="series-number">1394</span> “A picture (mental image, memory-image) of longing.” One thinks one has already done enough by talking about a ‘picture’; because longing is precisely a content of consciousness, and its image is something that is (very) similar to it, albeit more indistinct than the original. And one could certainly say of someone who is playing longing in the theater that he experiences, or has, an image of longing: namely not as an _explanation_ of his actions, but as a description of them.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#358.6+359.1) <span class="fac">[358\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/358.webp),[359\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/359.webp)</span> {#ts-229-358-6--359-1}

<span class="series-number">1395</span> But would I not still say that the actor experiences something similar to real longing? Is there not something to what James says: that the emotional movement consists of the feelings of the body, and therefore can, at least partially, be reproduced through voluntary movements?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#359.2) <span class="fac">[359\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/359.webp)</span> {#ts-229-359-2}

<span class="series-number">1396</span> Is pulling down the corners of the mouth so unpleasant, so sad, and pulling them up so pleasant? What is it that is so terrible about fear? The trembling, the rapid breathing, the feeling in the facial muscles? – If you say, “This fear, this uncertainty is terrible!” – could you continue: “If only there wasn’t this feeling in my stomach!”?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#359.3) <span class="fac">[359\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/359.webp)</span> {#ts-229-359-3}

<span class="series-number">1397</span> The expression “This fear is terrible!” is similar to a groan, a scream. If asked, “Why are you screaming?” – would we not point to the stomach, the chest, etc., as in the case of pain; but perhaps to what the fear provokes.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#359.4) <span class="fac">[359\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/359.webp)</span> {#ts-229-359-4}

<span class="series-number">1398</span> If fear is terrible, and if I am conscious of my breathing and a tension in my facial muscles, – does that mean that _these feelings_ are terrible to me? Could they not even mean a relief?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#359.5) <span class="fac">[359\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/359.webp)</span> {#ts-229-359-5}

<span class="series-number">1399</span> Compare fear and anxiety with worry.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#359.6+360.1) <span class="fac">[359\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/359.webp),[360\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/360.webp)</span> {#ts-229-359-6--360-1}

<span class="series-number">1400</span> And what is _that_ for a description: “Eternal gloom descends…”? One could describe a pain in this way; yes, even paint it.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#360.2) <span class="fac">[360\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/360.webp)</span> {#ts-229-360-2}

<span class="series-number">1401</span> Isn't the ‘content’ what populates the space of sensation? What changes, proceeds in space and time. If one speaks to oneself, then it would be the imagined sounds (and perhaps feelings in the throat, or the like).


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#360.3) <span class="fac">[360\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/360.webp)</span> {#ts-229-360-3}

<span class="series-number">1402</span> Is lying a specific experience? Well, can I say to someone, “I am going to lie to you now,” and then do it?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#360.4) <span class="fac">[360\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/360.webp)</span> {#ts-229-360-4}

<span class="series-number">1403</span> In what way am I conscious of the lie while I am lying? Only insofar as it does not only come to my consciousness later, and I later know that I lied. Being conscious of the lie is a _capability_. This does not contradict the fact that there are characteristic feelings of lying.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#360.5) <span class="fac">[360\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/360.webp)</span> {#ts-229-360-5}

<span class="series-number">1404</span> Knowledge is not simply _translated_ into words when it is expressed. The words are not a translation of something else that was there before them.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#360.6+361.1) <span class="fac">[360\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/360.webp),[361\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/361.webp)</span> {#ts-229-360-6--361-1}

<span class="series-number">1405</span> One says, “I can tell from his tone that he doesn't believe what he is saying,” or I assume it because he has generally proven to be unreliable. How can I apply this to _myself_? Can I, for example, conclude from my tone that I will probably not act according to my words? (And yet the other person does.) Or can I conclude it from my past unreliability? The latter is more likely. But I don't judge the tone of my voice the same way I judge the tone of the other person. Yes, if I could see myself later, perhaps in a talking film, I might say, “I’m not quite sure of myself.”


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#361.2) <span class="fac">[361\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/361.webp)</span> {#ts-229-361-2}

<span class="series-number">1406</span> Above all, however: don't I have a substitute for all such conjectures, one that is more reliable than they are? I _know_ that I don't believe what I say, and that gives me the best reason – would I say – to assume that I will not act according to my words. Yes; I simply have an _intention_ regarding my actions.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#361.3) <span class="fac">[361\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/361.webp)</span> {#ts-229-361-3}

<span class="series-number">1407</span> “I know that I am lying! Why do I need to draw conclusions from my tone, etc.?” – But that is not the case. For the question is: Can I draw the same conclusions from this ‘knowledge,’ for example, about the future, can I make the same _application_ of it, as from the observed signs?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#361.4) <span class="fac">[361\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/361.webp)</span> {#ts-229-361-4}

<span class="series-number">1408</span> And is the intention always _completely_ clear? I say, for example, “It will probably work out” – _partly_ because I believe it, _partly_ because I want to comfort the other person.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#361.5) <span class="fac">[361\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/361.webp)</span> {#ts-229-361-5}

<span class="series-number">1409</span> Hidden motives. “I know my own, I _suspect_ his.” But what _interest_, what importance, do his hidden motives have for me? (Well, consider it.) And the ‘knowledge’ of my own hidden motives now plays exactly the same role for _me_, as the assumption of his hidden motives does for _him_.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#362.1) <span class="fac">[362\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/362.webp)</span> {#ts-229-362-1}

<span class="series-number">1410</span> ‘Judging oneself.’ That of course exists. And I sometimes conclude that the other person is in pain because he behaves as I would in that case.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#362.2) <span class="fac">[362\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/362.webp)</span> {#ts-229-362-2}

<span class="series-number">1411</span> One could say: If I tell you my hidden motives, then I am telling you precisely what you would suspect if you were suspecting the hidden motives. That is: if you suspect the hidden motives, as it were, as an active principle, and I express them, then you can use my utterance directly to describe that agent. My utterance explains precisely what he wants to explain.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#362.3) <span class="fac">[362\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/362.webp)</span> {#ts-229-362-3}

<span class="series-number">1412</span> “Why should I draw conclusions about my behaviour from my own words, when I know anyway what I believe?” And how does it manifest that I know what I believe? Doesn't it manifest in the fact that I don't draw conclusions about my behaviour from my words? That is the fact.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#362.4) <span class="fac">[362\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/362.webp)</span> {#ts-229-362-4}

<span class="series-number">1413</span> Why don't I conclude from my tone that I don't really believe what I'm saying? or about all the things one concludes from that latter? – And if one answers, “Because I know my _conviction_,” then the question is, “How does that show itself?” Should I say: “In the fact that I don't doubt what it is”?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#362.5) <span class="fac">[362\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/362.webp)</span> {#ts-229-362-5}

<span class="series-number">1414</span> The _knowledge_ of the meter. Whoever _knows_ the meter hears it differently.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#363.1) <span class="fac">[363\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/363.webp)</span> {#ts-229-363-1}

<span class="series-number">1415</span> There are worrying thoughts, but not toothache-inducing ones.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#363.2) <span class="fac">[363\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/363.webp)</span> {#ts-229-363-2}

<span class="series-number">1416</span> I am whistling a note now, but also a melody now.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#363.3) <span class="fac">[363\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/363.webp)</span> {#ts-229-363-3}

<span class="series-number">1417</span> We don't say: “I look angry; I just hope I don't commit an act of violence.” But the question is not: “How does that come about?”


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#363.4) <span class="fac">[363\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/363.webp)</span> {#ts-229-363-4}

<span class="series-number">1418</span> The psychology of judgment. For judgment also has its psychology. It is important that one can imagine that every judgment begins with the word “I.” “I judge that ‥‥” So is every judgment then about the one who is judging? Not in so far as I do not want the main consequences to be drawn about _myself_, but about the object of the judgment. If I say, “It is raining,” I generally do not want one to respond, “So it _seems_ to _you_.” “We are talking about the weather,” I could say, “not about me.”


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#363.5) <span class="fac">[363\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/363.webp)</span> {#ts-229-363-5}

<span class="series-number">1419</span> “But why is the use of the verb ‘to believe,’ its grammar, in so strange a way composed?” Well, it is not _strangely_ composed. It is only strange if one compares it with the grammar of the word “to eat,” for example.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#363.6+364.1) <span class="fac">[363\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/363.webp),[364\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/364.webp)</span> {#ts-229-363-6--364-1}

<span class="series-number">1420</span> “What will he probably do now,” I say, while watching him. If I consider myself, and say, “What will I probably do now?”


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#364.2) <span class="fac">[364\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/364.webp)</span> {#ts-229-364-2}

<span class="series-number">1421</span> Suppose I move in a room and have a light screen in front of my eyes, on which I see myself, as an observer would see me. While I move around the room, I always look only at the screen and observe my actions on it. – What would then be the difference between the two cases: a) I am guided by what I see on the light screen, as by normal seeing of my surroundings – – b) I move _involuntarily_ and observe myself as if I were a stranger. But don’t I feel my movements? – But isn’t this feeling happening to me, just like any other sense impression?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#364.3) <span class="fac">[364\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/364.webp)</span> {#ts-229-364-3}

<span class="series-number">1422</span> Very well: the kinesthetic is something different, a special feeling. – But so are smell, hearing, etc. – Why does this make such a difference? “Feeling of innervation” – that expresses what one wants to say: that it is like an _Impuls_. But a feeling like an impulse?! What is an impulse? A physical **picture**. The picture of a shock.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#364.4+365.1) <span class="fac">[364\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/364.webp),[365\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/365.webp)</span> {#ts-229-364-4--365-1}

<span class="series-number">1423</span> What is the difference between these two: Following a line involuntarily – – Following a line with intention. What is the difference between these two: Tracing a line carefully and with great attention – – Attentively observing how my hand follows a line.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#365.2) <span class="fac">[365\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/365.webp)</span> {#ts-229-365-2}

<span class="series-number">1424</span> Certain differences are easy to indicate. One lies in the anticipation of what the hand will do.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#365.3) <span class="fac">[365\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/365.webp)</span> {#ts-229-365-3}

<span class="series-number">1425</span> Is “I do my best” the expression of an experience? – _One_ difference: One says, “Do your best!”


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#365.4) <span class="fac">[365\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/365.webp)</span> {#ts-229-365-4}

<span class="series-number">1426</span> Does one say, “Give yourself this muscular feeling!”? And why not? – “This”? – Which one? – – But can’t I give myself a specific muscular feeling by simply moving my arm? – Try it! Move your arm, and ask yourself what feeling you have evoked. If someone were to say to me, “Bend your arm and evoke the characteristic feeling,” and I bend my arm, then I would have to ask him: “Which one did you mean? A slight tension in the biceps, or a feeling in the skin on the inside of the elbow joint?” Yes, I could, if someone were to command me to do a movement, do it, and then describe the sensations it brings about and their particular location (which would almost never be the joint). And I would also often have to say that I have felt _nothing_. One must only not confuse this with the statement that it was as if my limb were _numb_.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#366.1) <span class="fac">[366\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/366.webp)</span> {#ts-229-366-1}

<span class="series-number">1427</span> Do you read the page arbitrarily? And what is the _act_ here? – Someone can read on command and stop reading. One can also imagine something on command. For example, recite a poem in one’s imagination, do a calculation. Do you _feel_, when imagining, whether you are imagining something arbitrarily or involuntarily? One can evoke thoughts on command, evoke images – but also, and this is something else, think something on command, imagine something.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#366.2) <span class="fac">[366\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/366.webp)</span> {#ts-229-366-2}

<span class="series-number">1428</span> Images, one could say, are voluntary; afterimages are involuntary.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#366.3) <span class="fac">[366\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/366.webp)</span> {#ts-229-366-3}

<span class="series-number">1429</span> Involuntary is, _e.g._, the movement that one cannot prevent; or the one of which one knows nothing; or the one that occurs when one deliberately relaxes one’s muscles in order not to influence the movement.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#366.4) <span class="fac">[366\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/366.webp)</span> {#ts-229-366-4}

<span class="series-number">1430</span> When I see the other person eating, do I ask myself whether he is doing it voluntarily or involuntarily? One might say that I simply assume that it is happening voluntarily. What do I assume; that he feels it? And that he feels it in a certain way?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#366.5) <span class="fac">[366\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/366.webp)</span> {#ts-229-366-5}

<span class="series-number">1431</span> How do I know whether the child is eating, drinking, walking, etc. voluntarily or involuntarily? Do I ask him what he feels? No; eating, as everyone eats, _is_ voluntary.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#367.1) <span class="fac">[367\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/367.webp)</span> {#ts-229-367-1}

<span class="series-number">1432</span> If someone were to tell us, _he_ is eating involuntarily, – what evidence would make me believe this?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#367.2) <span class="fac">[367\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/367.webp)</span> {#ts-229-367-2}

<span class="series-number">1433</span> If I suddenly raise my hand to protect my eye, – is the movement voluntary? – and do I feel it differently than a voluntary one?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#367.3) <span class="fac">[367\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/367.webp)</span> {#ts-229-367-3}

<span class="series-number">1434</span> The concept of ‘effort’. Do you feel the effort? Of course, you feel it. But don’t you also _make_ it? – What are the signs of effort? I lift a heavy weight with great effort. My muscles are tense, my face is contorted, my breath is held – – but do I do this; or is it simply happening to me? What if it were to happen to me now? How would that differ from the case of willing? Would I speak differently? Would I say: “I don’t know what’s happening to me: my muscles are tense, my face etc. etc.”? And if I said: “Well, relax your muscles,” he would reply, “I can’t.” But what if someone said to me: “I feel that I _must_ do whatever I do,” and that he behaves like anyone else?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#367.4) <span class="fac">[367\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/367.webp)</span> {#ts-229-367-4}

<span class="series-number">1435</span> Isn’t it the case that saying that the kinesthetic sensation shows me the movement that is being made is analogous to the view that a characteristic of pain shows me its location?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#368.1) <span class="fac">[368\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/368.webp)</span> {#ts-229-368-1}

<span class="series-number">1436</span> If someone wanted to represent pain through a _color image_, – would he include a vocal sign in the image? And why not?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#368.2) <span class="fac">[368\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/368.webp)</span> {#ts-229-368-2}

<span class="series-number">1437</span> Isn’t sensation the measure of effort? That is, when I say “I am pulling harder now,” do I notice it in the degree of sensation? And what is to be said against this? One says to someone “Exert yourself more!” – not in order for him to feel more, but to achieve more.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#368.3) <span class="fac">[368\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/368.webp)</span> {#ts-229-368-3}

<span class="series-number">1438</span> Why does one feel that one can describe a tactile sensation (its content), paint it, but not a movement or position sensation?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#368.4) <span class="fac">[368\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/368.webp)</span> {#ts-229-368-4}

<span class="series-number">1439</span> Can you, for example, say that your position sensation is weak or strong? And your sensations during the movement of a limb may be stronger or weaker (or absent), but that is not a perception of the movement.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#368.5) <span class="fac">[368\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/368.webp)</span> {#ts-229-368-5}

<span class="series-number">1440</span> Movement sensations – these are sensations that are caused by movements – can, for example, be pain. How does one know that it is not these movement sensations that teach us how to move? What would be a sign that this is _the_ case?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#369.1) <span class="fac">[369\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/369.webp)</span> {#ts-229-369-1}

<span class="series-number">1441</span> Isn’t it an important fact that the theater shows us colors and sounds, but not tactile sensations? One could imagine the use of smells and temperature sensations, but not the use of tactile sensations.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#369.2) <span class="fac">[369\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/369.webp)</span> {#ts-229-369-2}

<span class="series-number">1442</span> Someone, who with apparent attention threads a needle and tells us that he is doing it _involuntarily_. How could he justify this statement?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#369.3) <span class="fac">[369\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/369.webp)</span> {#ts-229-369-3}

<span class="series-number">1443</span> One can be convinced of what one knows – and one can also guess it. (Grammatical remark.)


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#369.4) <span class="fac">[369\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/369.webp)</span> {#ts-229-369-4}

<span class="series-number">1444</span> Certain movements, with their normal _environment_, are associated with intention, learning, trying, and acting. Movements for which it makes sense to say that they are sometimes voluntary and sometimes involuntary are movements in a specific environment.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#369.5) <span class="fac">[369\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/369.webp)</span> {#ts-229-369-5}

<span class="series-number">1445</span> A category of psychological phenomena (facts) would be ‘_germs_’. But this word can just as easily be the expression of a misunderstanding as the word “tendency experience” (James). The word “board game move” also does not characterize a _type of movement_.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#369.6+370.1) <span class="fac">[369\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/369.webp),[370\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/370.webp)</span> {#ts-229-369-6--370-1}

<span class="series-number">1446</span> Translating from one language to another is a mathematical task, and translating a lyrical poem, for example, into a foreign language is analogous to a mathematical _problem_. One can, after all, pose the problem of “How is this joke (for example) to be translated into a joke in the other language,” i.e., to be replaced; and the problem _can_ also be solved; but there was no method, no system, for solving it.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#370.2) <span class="fac">[370\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/370.webp)</span> {#ts-229-370-2}

<span class="series-number">1447</span> You know that you are lying; you know it when you lie. An inner voice, a feeling, tells me this? Couldn’t this feeling deceive me? Does it always tell me with a voice? And when does it speak? All the time? – And how do I know that I can trust it?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#370.3) <span class="fac">[370\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/370.webp)</span> {#ts-229-370-3}

<span class="series-number">1448</span> A lie has a special environment. There is, above all, a motive. An inducement.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#370.4) <span class="fac">[370\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/370.webp)</span> {#ts-229-370-4}

<span class="series-number">1449</span> The consciousness of lying is of the same category as the consciousness of intention.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#370.5) <span class="fac">[370\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/370.webp)</span> {#ts-229-370-5}

<span class="series-number">1450</span> Don’t forget: sight, hearing, smell, taste, etc., are only sensations because these concepts have _something_ in common – – just as one could group together drills, chisels, axes, and gas-powered blowtorches, because they have _certain_ functions in common.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#370.6+371.1) <span class="fac">[370\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/370.webp),[371\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/371.webp)</span> {#ts-229-370-6--371-1}

<span class="series-number">1451</span> “Pain, tone, taste, smell, has a specific color.” What does that mean? (Quality. Adjective.) A color can be green**ish** or blue**ish** – there is a mixture of colors; and so also a mixture of smells, sounds, tastes; qualitative intermediate stages. How does one distinguish qualitative from quantitative intermediate stages, I mean, from stages of ‘intensity’!? Still bearable – no longer bearable, these are, for example, degrees of intensity. Consider, someone asked: “How can I know whether what I experience as different degrees, of loudness for example, the other person experiences as different qualities, comparable to different colors?” – Compare the reaction to a change in intensity with the reaction to a change in quality.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#371.2) <span class="fac">[371\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/371.webp)</span> {#ts-229-371-2}

<span class="series-number">1452</span> I feel my arm, and, strangely enough, I would now like to say: I feel it in space in a certain position; as if the body sensation were distributed in space in the form of the arm, so that, in order to represent it, I would have to represent the arm, for example in plaster, in its correct position.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#371.3) <span class="fac">[371\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/371.webp)</span> {#ts-229-371-3}

<span class="series-number">1453</span> Imagine that a pencil tip is brought into contact with my skin at some point; I can then say that I feel where it is. But do I feel _where_ I feel it? “How do you know that the tip is now touching your thigh?” – “I feel it.” By feeling the touch, I know its location; but should I therefore speak of a sense of location? And if there is no sense of location, why should there be a feeling of position?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#372.1) <span class="fac">[372\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/372.webp)</span> {#ts-229-372-1}

<span class="series-number">1454</span> Yes, it is odd. My forearm is now lying horizontally, and I would like to say that I feel this; but not in such a way that I have a feeling that always goes together with this position (as if one were feeling, for example, a lack of blood or plethora) – but as if the ‘body sensation’ of the arm were arranged or distributed horizontally, like a mist or dust particles distributed in space on the surface of my arm. So it is not really as if I feel the position of my arm, but as if I feel my _arm_, and the feeling has this or that _position_. That is to say: I simply _know_ how it lies – without knowing it, _because_… How I also know where I feel the pain – but do not know it, _because_…


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#372.2) <span class="fac">[372\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/372.webp)</span> {#ts-229-372-2}

<span class="series-number">1455</span> Consider: – “It is not true that I always believe the wrong thing. For example, it is raining now, and I believe it.” One could say of him: He speaks as _two_ people.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#372.3) <span class="fac">[372\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/372.webp)</span> {#ts-229-372-3}

<span class="series-number">1456</span> Why do I have doubts about his intention, but not about my own? In what way do I know my intention beyond any doubt? What is, so to speak, the use of me _knowing_ my intention? What, namely, is the use, the function, of the expression of intention? When, namely, is it an expression of intention? Indeed, when the deed follows it, when it is a prediction. I make the prediction, the same prediction that the other makes from observing my behaviour, _without_ this observation.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#373.1) <span class="fac">[373\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/373.webp)</span> {#ts-229-373-1}

<span class="series-number">1457</span> If it is a matter of a ‘feeling of unreality’, we are inclined to say: “All I know is that people often say, under certain circumstances, that they feel that everything around them is ‘unreal’. We also know, of course, how the use of this word has been conditioned, and also something about their other utterances. That is all we know.” – Why don’t we also speak like this when it comes to the utterance of pleasure, conviction, the arbitrariness and involuntariness of movements?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#373.2) <span class="fac">[373\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/373.webp)</span> {#ts-229-373-2}

<span class="series-number">1458</span> What should I reply to someone who tells me that _he_ feels the position and movement of his limbs, that _he_ has a _feeling_ of their position and movement? Should I say that he is lying, or that he is mistaken, or should I believe him? I would like to ask him _how_ a feeling teaches him this position, for example. Or better: how he _knows_ that his _feeling_ teaches him this.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#373.3) <span class="fac">[373\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/373.webp)</span> {#ts-229-373-3}

<span class="series-number">1459</span> (One says the _usual_ thing – with the wrong gesture.)


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#373.4+374.1) <span class="fac">[373\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/373.webp),[374\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/374.webp)</span> {#ts-229-373-4--374-1}

<span class="series-number">1460</span> Remember here again the feeling, without justification and, on the surface, without reason, that a certain location must be in _that_ direction. If this feeling did not mostly deceive us, one would speak of a feeling-based _knowledge_ here. And the sources of this feeling can only be _conjectured_, or established empirically.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#374.2) <span class="fac">[374\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/374.webp)</span> {#ts-229-374-2}

<span class="series-number">1461</span> The most important thing here is that one can be aware of a difference, which is a categorical one, without being able to say in what the difference consists. This is the case in which one usually says that one recognizes the difference through introspection.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#374.3) <span class="fac">[374\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/374.webp)</span> {#ts-229-374-3}

<span class="series-number">1462</span> And yet it sounds too much like an appeal to introspection if I were to say, “Try it – see if you really determine the position of your limbs by feelings in them!” – And it would also be wrong, because the question is: How would that show itself if someone did it? Because if, after a self-examination, he assured me that it was so, or that it was not so – how do I know whether I can trust him; I mean, whether he also understood me correctly. Or also: How do I test whether I understand him?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#374.4) <span class="fac">[374\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/374.webp)</span> {#ts-229-374-4}

<span class="series-number">1463</span> Someone says to me: “I do not know how I move my fingers, but I know that I spread them through the feeling in my interdigital membranes.” Here one would have to ask: Can you not simply carry out the command “Spread your fingers” with your eyes closed?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#375.1) <span class="fac">[375\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/375.webp)</span> {#ts-229-375-1}

<span class="series-number">1464</span> We feel our movements. Yes, we really _feel_ them; the sensation is not like a sensation of taste or heat, but rather like a sensation of touch: the sensation when skin and muscles are pressed, pulled, or shifted.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#375.2) <span class="fac">[375\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/375.webp)</span> {#ts-229-375-2}

<span class="series-number">1465</span> How can I make use of the sensation of movement in my movements? For how can I, before the movement has begun, select from all the muscles those that will give me the correct sensation of movement? – If it is a problem “How do I know, if I cannot see the movement, that it has taken place, and how far it has?” – why is it not a problem: “How do I know how to initiate the movement in the first place?” (Russell once made a false remark about this.)


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#375.3) <span class="fac">[375\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/375.webp)</span> {#ts-229-375-3}

<span class="series-number">1466</span> I can, for example, say that I now know that my finger is bent, but that I have no sensation in it; in any case, not one that I particularly associate with this position. If one were to ask me: “Do you feel anything that you would not feel in the extended position; or are you missing a sensation that would be present in the other position?” – then I would have to answer no.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#375.4+376.1) <span class="fac">[375\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/375.webp),[376\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/376.webp)</span> {#ts-229-375-4--376-1}

<span class="series-number">1467</span> “Is pleasure a sensation?” (I.A. Richards). That is, is pleasure something like a tone or a smell? – But is a tone something like a smell? In what respect?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#376.2) <span class="fac">[376\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/376.webp)</span> {#ts-229-376-2}

<span class="series-number">1468</span> Whoever asks whether pleasure is a sensation probably does not distinguish between ground and cause, for otherwise he would realize that one has pleasure _in_ something, which does not mean that this something causes a sensation in us.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#376.3) <span class="fac">[376\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/376.webp)</span> {#ts-229-376-3}

<span class="series-number">1469</span> But pleasure certainly goes hand in hand with a facial expression, and although we do not see it on ourselves, we do feel it. And try thinking about something very sad with a radiant expression of joy!


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#376.4) <span class="fac">[376\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/376.webp)</span> {#ts-229-376-4}

<span class="series-number">1470</span> It is possible that the glands of the sad person secrete differently than those of the happy person; also, that this secretion is the, or one of the, causes of sadness. But does it follow that sadness is a sensation caused by this secretion?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#376.5+377.1) <span class="fac">[376\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/376.webp),[377\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/377.webp)</span> {#ts-229-376-5--377-1}

<span class="series-number">1471</span> But the thought here is: “You _feel_ sadness – so you must feel it _somewhere_; otherwise, it would be a chimera.” But if you want to think this, just recall the difference between seeing and pain. I feel the pain in my hand – and the color in my eye? In the way we want to use a schema here, instead of simply noting what is actually similar, we see everything simplified incorrectly.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#377.2) <span class="fac">[377\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/377.webp)</span> {#ts-229-377-2}

<span class="series-number">1472</span> A smell can be very pleasant. Is the pleasantness of it merely a sensation? Then the sensation of pleasantness would accompany the smell. But how would it _relate_ to it? Certainly, the expression of pleasantness is similar in _kind_ to the expression of a sensation, especially pain. But joy has no location; there are joyful thoughts, but not toothache. But – one might say – whether joy is a sensation or _what_ it is, one must notice when one has it! – (And why especially when one _has_ it, and not when one _does not have_ it?) Do you also notice the _essence_ of one when you eat _an_ apple, and the _essence_ of zero when you eat none?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#377.3) <span class="fac">[377\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/377.webp)</span> {#ts-229-377-3}

<span class="series-number">1473</span> Arbitrariness is connected with intention. And therefore also with resolve. One does not resolve to have a heart cramp and then have it.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#377.4) <span class="fac">[377\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/377.webp)</span> {#ts-229-377-4}

<span class="series-number">1474</span> One can induce a sneeze or a coughing fit, but not an arbitrary movement. And the will does not induce sneezing, nor does it induce walking.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#378.1) <span class="fac">[378\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/378.webp)</span> {#ts-229-378-1}

<span class="series-number">1475</span> Sensation, that is what one considers to be immediately given and concrete, what one only needs to look at in order to recognize it; what is actually there. (The thing, not its representative.)


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#378.2) <span class="fac">[378\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/378.webp)</span> {#ts-229-378-2}

<span class="series-number">1476</span> “I know whether I am speaking in accordance with my conviction, or contrary to it.” Thus, the conviction is the important thing. In the background of my utterances. What a _powerful_ image. One could paint conviction and speech. (“from the deepest breast”). And yet, how little does this image show!


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#378.3) <span class="fac">[378\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/378.webp)</span> {#ts-229-378-3}

<span class="series-number">1477</span> “The smell is wonderful!” Is there any doubt that it is the smell that is wonderful? So is it a property of the smell? – Why not? It is a property that ten is divisible by two, and also, that it is the number of my fingers. But there could be a language in which people simply close their eyes and say “Oh, this smell!” and there is no subject-predicate sentence that is equivalent to it. That is simply a ‘specific’ reaction.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#378.4) <span class="fac">[378\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/378.webp)</span> {#ts-229-378-4}

<span class="series-number">1478</span> Is what he says he has, & what I say I have, without our inferring this from any observation, the same as what we infer from observing the other’s **behaviour** & from his expression of conviction?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#379.1) <span class="fac">[379\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/379.webp)</span> {#ts-229-379-1}

<span class="series-number">1479</span> Can one say: I _infer_ that he will act as he _intends_ to act?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#379.2) <span class="fac">[379\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/379.webp)</span> {#ts-229-379-2}

<span class="series-number">1480</span> I infer the consequences of his conviction from the expression of his conviction; but not the consequences of my conviction from its expression.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#379.3) <span class="fac">[379\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/379.webp)</span> {#ts-229-379-3}

<span class="series-number">1481</span> Imagine an observer who, as if automatically, utters his observations. Yes, he hears himself speak, but he does not, as it were, take note of it. He sees that the enemy is approaching and reports it, describes it, but like a machine. How would that be? Well, he does not act according to his observation. One could say that he expresses what he sees, but he does not _believe_ it. It does not, as it were, penetrate him.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#379.4) <span class="fac">[379\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/379.webp)</span> {#ts-229-379-4}

<span class="series-number">1482</span> Why do I not infer a state from my own words, from which words and actions spring? Above all, I do not infer my probable actions from my words.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#379.5) <span class="fac">[379\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/379.webp)</span> {#ts-229-379-5}

<span class="series-number">1483</span> Asked “Will you act that way?” – I consider _reasons_ and _counter-reasons_.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#379.6+380.1) <span class="fac">[379\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/379.webp),[380\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/380.webp)</span> {#ts-229-379-6--380-1}

<span class="series-number">1484</span> But consider: “I sometimes take the other person's word – so I must at least sometimes also take my own for it, that I have this or that conviction. But if, as it were automatically, I report my observation, then this report has nothing to do with my conviction. However, I could trust myself, or my observing self, just as much as another person does. I could therefore say: “I say ‘it is raining’, so it probably is”. Or: “The observer in me says ‘it is raining’, and I am inclined to believe him.” – Is it not so – or similar – when a person says that God has spoken to him, or through his mouth?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#380.2) <span class="fac">[380\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/380.webp)</span> {#ts-229-380-2}

<span class="series-number">1485</span> The _important_ insight is that there is a language-game in which I, _automatically_, make a communication, which can be treated by others in exactly the same way as a non-automatic one – only that here there will be no talk of ‘lying’ – a communication that I myself can receive as if it came from a third party. One could also call the ‘automatic’ statement, message, etc., an ‘oracle’. – This means, of course, that the oracle should not use the words “I believe …”.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#380.3) <span class="fac">[380\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/380.webp)</span> {#ts-229-380-3}

<span class="series-number">1486</span> Where in logic does it say that an assertion is not allowed to be made in a trance?!


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#380.4) <span class="fac">[380\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/380.webp)</span> {#ts-229-380-4}

<span class="series-number">1487</span> “If I look out, I see that it is raining; if I look into myself, I see that I do not believe it.” And what are we to do with this communication?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#381.1) <span class="fac">[381\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/381.webp)</span> {#ts-229-381-1}

<span class="series-number">1488</span> “Let’s assume it’s raining, and I don’t believe it” – if I assert what this assumption assumes, then, as it were, my personality splits. “Then my personality splits” means: then I’m no longer playing the usual language-game, but another one.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#381.2) <span class="fac">[381\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/381.webp)</span> {#ts-229-381-2}

<span class="series-number">1489</span> The words “It is raining” are written in his mind – this is meant to mean (i.e., to be replaceable by) “He believes that it is raining.” “The words ‘It is raining’ are written in my mind” – this means something like: “I cannot rid myself of the belief that …,” “The idea has taken possession of me, that ….” Note that the words “I believe it is raining” and “It probably is raining” _**the same**_ can say: _insofar_ as, in certain contexts, it makes no difference which of the two sentences we use. (And free yourself from the idea that one is accompanied by a different mental process than the other!) The two sentences can say the same thing, although the first corresponds to “I believe …,” “He believes …,” etc., while the second does not. The first is simply formed with a different _concept_. That is: in order to say that it might be raining, we do not need the concept “believe,” although we can use it for that purpose. The concept that a sentence is ‘written in one’s mind’ is now a third concept, which in its application partially overlaps with the others, and partially does not. I mean that to form the statement “It probably is …,” one does not need the ‘strange’ concept ‘believe,’ although one can use it.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#381.3) <span class="fac">[381\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/381.webp)</span> {#ts-229-381-3}

<span class="series-number">1490</span> Also consider: “It probably is raining and it is raining” means nothing, and likewise “It probably is raining and it is not raining”. On the other hand, one can say “It seems to be raining and it is raining” and also “It seems to be raining and it is not raining”. And “It seems to be raining” can have the same sense as “It probably is raining”.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#382.1) <span class="fac">[382\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/382.webp)</span> {#ts-229-382-1}

<span class="series-number">1491</span> How do I know that I am in a state of belief; …? Do I look _into_ myself? Yes, does it help me _in any way_ if I observe myself? Well, I could ask myself: “How much would I bet in this case?”


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#382.2) <span class="fac">[382\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/382.webp)</span> {#ts-229-382-2}

<span class="series-number">1492</span> Pretending. Feigning pain. It does not simply consist of uttering an expression of pain without actually feeling pain. There must be a motive for the pretense, i.e., a situation that is not simply describable. Pretending to be sick and weak in order to then attack the person who is trying to help. – “But there is an _inner_ difference!” Of course; only “inner” is here a dangerous metaphor. – But the ‘proof’ that there is an inner difference is that I can _confess_ that I pretended. I confess an intention. Does it ‘_follow_’ from this that the intention was something inner?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#382.3) <span class="fac">[382\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/382.webp)</span> {#ts-229-382-3}

<span class="series-number">1493</span> The ‘truly infinite’ is a ‘mere word’. It would be better to say: this expression provisionally creates only a picture – which is still in the air; the application of which you still owe us.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#382.4) <span class="fac">[382\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/382.webp)</span> {#ts-229-382-4}

<span class="series-number">1494</span> An infinitely long row of spheres, an infinitely long rod. Imagine, let us say, that this is the subject of a kind of fairy tale. What application, even if only fictional, could one make of this concept? The question now is not: can such a thing exist? But: what do we imagine? So let your imagination really run wild! You can have it now, however you want. You only need to _say_ how you want it. So just create a word picture; illustrate it however you want – with drawings, with comparisons, etc.! You can, as it were, draw up a blueprint. And now the question is, how can we work according to it.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#382.5+383.1) <span class="fac">[382\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/382.webp),[383\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/383.webp)</span> {#ts-229-382-5--383-1}

<span class="series-number">1495</span> “But how can the human mind outstrip reality, and even _think_ about what is unverifiable?” – Why shouldn’t we _talk_ about what is unverifiable? We ourselves made it unverifiable. Does it create a false _appearance_? And how can it even _seem_ to do so? Do you want to say that this _state of affairs_ is not even a description? Well, then it is not a _false_ appearance, but rather one that deprives us of our orientation. So that we ask: How is it possible?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#383.2) <span class="fac">[383\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/383.webp)</span> {#ts-229-383-2}

<span class="series-number">1496</span> As soon as the word was spoken, I wished I hadn’t said it. – How did my wish relate to the spoken word? I felt that the word was inappropriate as soon as I had spoken it. But the signs that I remember were only like faint hints. Small things from which I could perhaps have guessed the intention, the wish, etc. There are shamanic occasions – situations – and shameful behaviour. As there are occasions for expectations and behaviours related to expectations.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#383.3) <span class="fac">[383\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/383.webp)</span> {#ts-229-383-3}

<span class="series-number">1497</span> If a cat is lurking in front of the mouse hole – do I assume that it is thinking about the mouse? If a robber is waiting for his victim, – does it involve him thinking about that person? Must he think about this and that? Compare the one who does this for the first time with one who has done it countless times! (read)


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#383.4) <span class="fac">[383\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/383.webp)</span> {#ts-229-383-4}

<span class="series-number">1498</span> There could be a verb that means: to express the intention through words, or other signs, aloud, or in thought. This verb would not be synonymous with our “to intend.” There could be a verb that means: to act according to an intention; and this would also not be synonymous with “to intend.” Another one could mean: to brood over an intention: or, to mull it over in one’s head.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#384.1) <span class="fac">[384\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/384.webp)</span> {#ts-229-384-1}

<span class="series-number">1499</span> When I prepare my coffee, I intend to drink it. If I were to prepare it now without this intention – would there be something _**missing**_ from this action? Is there something happening during the normal course of doing it that characterizes it as an action done with this intention? But if one were to ask me whether I intend to drink it, and I answered “yes, of course!” – would I be saying something about my current _state_? _So_ I react in this case; and _that_ can be inferred from my reaction.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#384.2) <span class="fac">[384\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/384.webp)</span> {#ts-229-384-2}

<span class="series-number">1500</span> One can call a belief, a wish, a fear, a hope, an affection a state of a person; one can rely on this state in one’s behavior towards this person, draw conclusions about their reactions from their state. And if someone says “I have been in this belief all this time ‥‥”, “I have harbored this wish throughout my life ‥‥”, etc., then he is reporting on a state, an attitude. – But if he says “I believe he is coming” (or simply “There he comes”) or “I wish you would come” (or simply “Please come!”) then he is acting, speaking, in accordance with that _state_, and is not reporting that he is in it. But if that were correct, then there should be a present form of those reports, that is, on the one hand, e.g., the _utterance_ “I believe ‥‥”, on the other hand, a _report_ “I am in the belief ‥‥”. And similarly for the wish, the intention, fear, etc.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#384.3) <span class="fac">[384\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/384.webp)</span> {#ts-229-384-3}

<span class="series-number">1501</span> Someone could say: “I remember my state in those years very precisely; whenever I was asked…, I answered…; that was my attitude.”


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#384.4) <span class="fac">[384\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/384.webp)</span> {#ts-229-384-4}

<span class="series-number">1502</span> There is a disgust **reaction**, in me & in the other; there are also _disgust feelings_. And in this respect, disgust, fear, affection, etc. are similar; but not hope, faith, etc.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#385.1) <span class="fac">[385\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/385.webp)</span> {#ts-229-385-1}

<span class="series-number">1503</span> Gram repeatedly dwells on the sad thought. A thought can be sad, disgusting, delightful, etc.; but how does the expression show that it is this thought to which we react in this way? How does one ward off a thought?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#385.2) <span class="fac">[385\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/385.webp)</span> {#ts-229-385-2}

<span class="series-number">1504</span> Should one call the entire area of the psychological the area of ‘experience’? So, for example, all psychological verbs are ‘experience verbs’. (‘experience concepts’.) Their characteristic is that their third person is expressed on the basis of observations, but not the first person. This observation is an observation of behaviour. A subclass of experience concepts are the ‘experience concepts’. ‘Experiences’ have duration, a course; they can proceed uniformly or nonuniformly. They have intensity. They are not characteristics of thoughts. Imagination is experience. A subclass of ‘experiences’ are ‘impressions’. Impressions have spatial and temporal relationships to each other. There are mixed impressions. For example, mixtures of smells, colours, sounds. ‘Moods’ are ‘experiences’, but are not ‘experiences’. (Examples: sorrow, joy, grief, delight.) And one could distinguish between ‘directed moods’ and ‘undirected moods’. The mood has duration; it has no location; it has characteristic experiences and thoughts; it has a characteristic _mimic_ expression. Thinking is speaking under certain circumstances, and other things corresponding to it. Moods colour thoughts. A subclass of ‘experiences’ are the forms of ‘conviction’. (Belief, certainty, doubt, etc.) Their expression is an expression of thoughts. They are not ‘colourings’ of thoughts. One could also call the directed moods ‘statements’. Surprise and fright are also statements, as are admiration, enjoyment.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#385.3+386.1) <span class="fac">[385\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/385.webp),[386\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/386.webp)</span> {#ts-229-385-3--386-1}

<span class="series-number">1505</span> But to what does memory and attention belong? One can remember a situation or event in an _instant_. In this respect, the concept of remembering is similar to that of immediate understanding, of deciding.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#386.2) <span class="fac">[386\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/386.webp)</span> {#ts-229-386-2}

<span class="series-number">1506</span> My behaviour is sometimes the object of my observation, but nevertheless _rarely_. And this is connected with the fact that I intend my behaviour. Even when the actor in the mirror observes his own expressions, or the musician pays close attention to every note of his playing and judges it, it is still done in order to direct his action accordingly.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#386.3) <span class="fac">[386\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/386.webp)</span> {#ts-229-386-3}

<span class="series-number">1507</span> What does it mean, for example, that self-observation makes my actions, my movements, uncertain? I cannot observe myself unobserved. And I do not observe myself for the same purpose as I observe others.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#386.4) <span class="fac">[386\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/386.webp)</span> {#ts-229-386-4}

<span class="series-number">1508</span> When a child stamps his feet and howls in anger, who would say that he does this involuntarily? And why? Why does one assume that he does not do this involuntarily? What are the _signs_ of voluntary action? Are there such signs? – What are the signs of involuntary movement? It does not follow commands, as voluntary action does. There is a “Come here!”, “Go there!”, “Make this arm movement!”; but not “Let your heart beat fast!”


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#386.5) <span class="fac">[386\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/386.webp)</span> {#ts-229-386-5}

<span class="series-number">1509</span> There is a certain interplay of movements, words, expressions, such as the expressions of unwillingness or willingness, which characterizes the voluntary movements of a normal person. When one calls the child, it does not come automatically: there are, for example, the gesture “I don’t want to!” Or the joyful coming, the decision to come, the running away with the sign of fear, the effects of persuasion, all the reactions of the game, the signs of consideration and its effects.
