### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#386.6+387.1) <span class="fac">[386\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/386.webp),[387\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/387.webp)</span> {#ts-229-386-6--387-1}

<span class="series-number">1510</span> A melody went through my head. Was it voluntary or involuntary? One answer would be: I could have stopped it, could have sung it internally. And how do I know that? Well, because I can usually interrupt myself when I want to.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#387.2) <span class="fac">[387\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/387.webp)</span> {#ts-229-387-2}

<span class="series-number">1511</span> How could I prove to myself that I can move my arm voluntarily? Perhaps, by saying to myself “I will move it now” and then it moves? Or should I say “Simply, by moving it”? But how do I know that I have done it and that it didn’t just move by chance? Do I feel it in the end? And what if my memory of past feelings deceives me, and so it wasn’t the right, decisive feelings at all?! (And which are the right ones?) And how does the other person know whether _I_ moved my arm voluntarily? I might tell him: “Tell me what movement you want, and I will make it, to convince you.” – And what do you feel in your arm? “Well, the usual.” There is nothing unusual about the feelings; the arm is not, for example, numb (as if it had ‘fallen asleep’).


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#387.3) <span class="fac">[387\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/387.webp)</span> {#ts-229-387-3}

<span class="series-number">1512</span> A movement of my body, of which I am not aware that it is happening, or has happened, will be called involuntary. – But what if I simply _try_ to lift a weight, so that the movement does not actually take place? What if someone strained involuntarily to lift a weight? Under what circumstances would one call _this_ behaviour ‘involuntary’?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#387.4) <span class="fac">[387\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/387.webp)</span> {#ts-229-387-4}

<span class="series-number">1513</span> Can not rest be just as voluntary as movement? Can the omission of movement be voluntary? What a better argument against an innervation feeling?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#387.5) <span class="fac">[387\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/387.webp)</span> {#ts-229-387-5}

<span class="series-number">1514</span> “This look was not intended” sometimes means: “I didn’t know that I was looking like that,” or “I didn’t mean anything by it.”


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#388.1) <span class="fac">[388\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/388.webp)</span> {#ts-229-388-1}

<span class="series-number">1515</span> It should not seem so obvious to us that memory shows us the past inner process in the same way as it shows us the past outer process.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#388.2) <span class="fac">[388\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/388.webp)</span> {#ts-229-388-2}

<span class="series-number">1516</span> Imagination is voluntary, memory is involuntary, but recalling something to memory is voluntary.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#388.3) <span class="fac">[388\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/388.webp)</span> {#ts-229-388-3}

<span class="series-number">1517</span> What a remarkable concept ‘trying’, ‘endeavouring’ is; what one can ‘try to do’! (Remember, lift a weight, pay attention, think of nothing.) But then one could also say: what a remarkable concept ‘doing’ is! What are the kinship relations between ‘speaking’ and ‘thinking’, between ‘speaking’ and ‘talking to oneself’? (Compare the kinship relations between the types of numbers.)


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#388.4) <span class="fac">[388\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/388.webp)</span> {#ts-229-388-4}

<span class="series-number">1518</span> One draws quite different conclusions from involuntary movement than from voluntary movement: this _characterizes_ voluntary movement.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#388.5) <span class="fac">[388\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/388.webp)</span> {#ts-229-388-5}

<span class="series-number">1519</span> But how do I know that this movement was voluntary? – I don’t know it, I express it.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#388.6) <span class="fac">[388\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/388.webp)</span> {#ts-229-388-6}

<span class="series-number">1520</span> “I pull as hard as I can.” How do I know that? Does my muscle feeling tell me? The words are a signal; and they have a _function_. But do I not _experience_ anything? Do I not experience something? something specific? A specific feeling of exertion and of not being able to go any further, of reaching the limit? Of course, but these expressions say no more than “I pull as hard as I can.”


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#388.7+389.1) <span class="fac">[388\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/388.webp),[389\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/389.webp)</span> {#ts-229-388-7--389-1}

<span class="series-number">1521</span> But it is important that all these paraphrases exist! That one can describe the worry with the words “Eternal gloom descends.” I may never have emphasized enough the importance of this paraphrasing. One depicts joy through a face illuminated by light, through rays emanating from it. Of course, that does not mean that joy and light are _similar_; but we associate – _regardless_ of why – joy with light. It _could_ be that this association is taught to the child when it learns to speak, that it is not _more natural_ than the sound of the words themselves – – enough, it exists. (“Beethoven” and Beethoven’s works.)


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#389.2) <span class="fac">[389\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/389.webp)</span> {#ts-229-389-2}

<span class="series-number">1522</span> Is grief similar to lead-gray skies?! And how can one find that out? By looking at the grieving person and the sky? Or does the grieving person say it? And is it then only true for _his_ grief, or for the grief of everyone?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#389.3) <span class="fac">[389\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/389.webp)</span> {#ts-229-389-3}

<span class="series-number">1523</span> If now someone says that _his_ grief is like a gray cloud, – should I believe it, or not? – One could ask him whether the two are similar _in something_, in a certain respect. (As, for example, two faces; or as a sudden, intense pain is to a flash of pain.) One can indicate relationships – internal relationships and _connections_ – of what one calls ‘intensities’ in different sensations.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#389.4) <span class="fac">[389\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/389.webp)</span> {#ts-229-389-4}

<span class="series-number">1524</span> ‘a is between b and c, and closer to b than to c’ this is a characteristic relation between sensations of the same kind. That is, there is, for example, a language-game with the command “Create a sensation between _this_ and _this_, and closer to the first than to the second!” And also: “Name two sensations, between which _this_ lies.”


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#389.5) <span class="fac">[389\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/389.webp)</span> {#ts-229-389-5}

<span class="series-number">1525</span> And it is important that, for example, with _gray_ one will get “black and white” as an answer; with _violet_ “blue and red”, with _pink_ “red and white”, etc.; but _not_ with _olive green_ “red and green”.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#389.6+390.1) <span class="fac">[389\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/389.webp),[390\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/390.webp)</span> {#ts-229-389-6--390-1}

<span class="series-number">1526</span> How does one recognize that the expression of joy is not the expression of bodily pain? (An _important_ question.)


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#390.2) <span class="fac">[390\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/390.webp)</span> {#ts-229-390-2}

<span class="series-number">1527</span> How does one know that the expression of pleasure is not the expression of a sensation?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#390.3) <span class="fac">[390\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/390.webp)</span> {#ts-229-390-3}

<span class="series-number">1528</span> To _address_ a figure as this or as that. Do you always address the figure, while you see it, as this or that? Of course: if asked what this figure represents, I would always say: “A rabbit”; but I am so little constantly conscious of it, as of the fact that this here is a real table. For if I always address a picture as the picture _of_ this object, then also every object as a thing of this particular use, etc.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#390.4) <span class="fac">[390\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/390.webp)</span> {#ts-229-390-4}

<span class="series-number">1529</span> If someone notices for the first time that the picture is ambiguous, he might react with the exclamation: “Ah, a rabbit!” etc.; but if he now sees the picture constantly in one aspect, he would not want to exclaim without interruption “Ah, a …!”


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#390.5) <span class="fac">[390\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/390.webp)</span> {#ts-229-390-5}

<span class="series-number">1530</span> I want to say that the natural, primitive expression of the experience of the aspect would be such an exclamation; it could also be a lighting up of the eyes. (Something strikes me!)


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#390.6) <span class="fac">[390\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/390.webp)</span> {#ts-229-390-6}

<span class="series-number">1531</span> If I say that I see this figure constantly red, this means that the description, that it is red – the description in words or through a picture – is constantly, without change, correct; in contrast, therefore, to _that_ case in which the figure changes. – The temptation is indeed to describe the aspect with the words “I see it _like this_” without pointing to anything. And if one describes a face with its direction of gaze as an arrow, then one wants to say: “I see this: and not this: 🖵”.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#390.7+391.1) <span class="fac">[390\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/390.webp),[391\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/391.webp)</span> {#ts-229-390-7--391-1}

<span class="series-number">1532</span> To the constant seeing as 🖵 corresponds then the fact that this description, without change, is the correct one and _that_ means only that the aspect has not changed.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#391.2) <span class="fac">[391\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/391.webp)</span> {#ts-229-391-2}

<span class="series-number">1533</span> Talk of hallucination! – What could be stranger than that the _point_, the eye, seems to have a direction!


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#391.3) <span class="fac">[391\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/391.webp)</span> {#ts-229-391-3}

<span class="series-number">1534</span> When I think about the facial expression of this figure, – how do I do it, in order to think about the expression of 🖵 and not of 🖵?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#391.4) <span class="fac">[391\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/391.webp)</span> {#ts-229-391-4}

<span class="series-number">1535</span> When I think about the facial expression of this figure, I look at it – how do I do it: to look at the expression of 🖵, not that of 🖵? And this symbolism, I think, already contains everything.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#391.5) <span class="fac">[391\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/391.webp)</span> {#ts-229-391-5}

<span class="series-number">1536</span> It is as if one were seeing the picture: sometimes together with _one_ group of pictures, at other times with _another_. What does it mean to say “It is as if one were seeing”? This means something similar to: _this_ process could represent the actual one, were it to have the right ‘multiplicity’.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#391.6) <span class="fac">[391\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/391.webp)</span> {#ts-229-391-6}

<span class="series-number">1537</span> It is – in contrast to Köhler – precisely a _meaning_ that one sees.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#391.7) <span class="fac">[391\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/391.webp)</span> {#ts-229-391-7}

<span class="series-number">1538</span> One could say that one experiences the _readiness_ for a certain group of thoughts. (The _seed_ of them.)


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#391.8) <span class="fac">[391\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/391.webp)</span> {#ts-229-391-8}

<span class="series-number">1539</span> It is as if the picture comes to rest in one position (or another). As if it could indeed fluctuate, and then come to rest with _certain_ accents. One says: “I see it now (or, mostly) as _this_.” It is really as if the lines were now joined together in _this_ form, and not in another. Or as if they had fallen into _this_ and not the other hollow. And yet it must only be a matter of describing the actual expression of our experience, which I am only paraphrasing with all these pictures; to say what the _essential_ thing about this expression is.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#392.1) <span class="fac">[392\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/392.webp)</span> {#ts-229-392-1}

<span class="series-number">1540</span> Could someone see the figure _this_ way, or _that_ way, without being able to proceed from it to explanations, etc.? Could someone see it _this_ way and _that_ way, without knowing what animal heads look like, what an eye is, etc.? And by this I do not mean: “Would such a person be able to do this, and would it succeed?” But: “Does it not require _these_ concepts?”


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#392.2) <span class="fac">[392\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/392.webp)</span> {#ts-229-392-2}

<span class="series-number">1541</span> I see the picture of a horse: I know not only that it is a horse, but also that the horse is running. So I can not only _understand_ the picture spatially, but I also _know_ what the horse is about to do. Imagine someone seeing a picture of a cavalry charge, but not knowing that the horses do not remain in their various positions! I am not concerned with an _explanation_ of this understanding, for example, by claiming that the observer makes small running movements, or feels running innervations. What reason does one have for making such assumptions, other than _the_ assumption that it ‘must’ be so?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#392.3) <span class="fac">[392\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/392.webp)</span> {#ts-229-392-3}

<span class="series-number">1542</span> But if one says “One _sees_ this painted horse running!” – I do not only want to say “I know that this represents a running horse.” One wants to say something _else_ with this. Imagine someone reacting to such a picture with a hand movement and the exclamation “Hui!”. Does that not say roughly the same thing as: he _sees_ the horse running? He could also _exclaim_ “It is running!” and that would not be the statement that it is running, nor that it _seems_ to be running. Just as one says: “Look how it runs!” – not to communicate something to the other, but it _is_ a reaction in which people _find_ themselves.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#393.1) <span class="fac">[393\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/393.webp)</span> {#ts-229-393-1}

<span class="series-number">1543</span> Understanding is similar to knowing something further, thus a skill: but “I understand”, just like “I know more”, is an _utterance_, a _signal_.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#393.2) <span class="fac">[393\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/393.webp)</span> {#ts-229-393-2}

<span class="series-number">1544</span> I can experience a word adjectivally, or substantively. Do I know whether everyone, whether many, with whom I talk, have these experiences? Would it be important in order to know what they mean?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#393.3) <span class="fac">[393\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/393.webp)</span> {#ts-229-393-3}

<span class="series-number">1545</span> I had not noticed that the same contour appeared in both pictures, because I had conceived of it in one picture _this_ way, in the other _that_ way 🖵. Only by a detour of thought did I realize that it was the same contour. – Is this proof: that each time I saw something _different_? – It is important that the two aspects are _incompatible_ with each other.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#393.4) <span class="fac">[393\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/393.webp)</span> {#ts-229-393-4}

<span class="series-number">1546</span> Is the facial expression something visual? I could imagine a picture, the _expression_ of which would be ambiguous. And that I would therefore not recognize it in a _different_ environment. I might say something like: “Oh yes, these are the same lines; but they look quite different here.” And I really _see_ that the picture 🖵 and the picture are the same. I don’t just recognize it, let’s say, by measurement!


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#394.1) <span class="fac">[394\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/394.webp)</span> {#ts-229-394-1}

<span class="series-number">1547</span> I see, you say, two different facial objects that only have something in common. Because you are thus emphasizing certain analogies at the expense of others. But this emphasis must now be grammatically justified.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#394.2) <span class="fac">[394\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/394.webp)</span> {#ts-229-394-2}

<span class="series-number">1548</span> How is it possible that the eye, this _point_, looks in one _direction_? – “_Look how it looks_!” (And in doing so, one ‘looks’ oneself.) But one does not say and do this continuously while looking at the picture. And what is this “Look how it looks!” – is it the expression of a sensation?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#394.3) <span class="fac">[394\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/394.webp)</span> {#ts-229-394-3}

<span class="series-number">1549</span> I would never have thought of bringing the two pictures _like this_ into alignment, of comparing them _like this_. Because they suggest a different way of comparison. The picture 🖵 also has not the slightest similarity with the picture 🖵, one might say – – although they are congruent.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#394.4) <span class="fac">[394\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/394.webp)</span> {#ts-229-394-4}

<span class="series-number">1550</span> “Now I understand more” – I see that _this_ is a forehead and _that_ is a beak. This line is forehead-like, this point eye-like. But how can the _visual impression_ of a line be forehead-like? And what is it that makes me say that the visual impression itself has this property? – Well, that it is not a thought, not an interpretation, that it is lasting, like the visual impression.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#395.1) <span class="fac">[395\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/395.webp)</span> {#ts-229-395-1}

<span class="series-number">1551</span> Let’s try to describe that people have intentions! What would such a description look like? _For whom_ would it be a description? Ask yourself this: What purpose should it serve?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#395.2) <span class="fac">[395\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/395.webp)</span> {#ts-229-395-2}

<span class="series-number">1552</span> One can very ‘clearly’ talk to oneself in one’s imagination, if one reproduces the information of speech by humming (with closed lips). Movements of the larynx also help. But the remarkable thing is that one then _hears_ the speech in one’s imagination, and not just, as it were, feels its skeleton in the larynx.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#395.3) <span class="fac">[395\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/395.webp)</span> {#ts-229-395-3}

<span class="series-number">1553</span> It is _essential_ for ‘imagining’ that the concepts of sensory perception are used in its expression. (The sentence “I hear and I do not hear ‥‥” could be used as an expression of auditory imagination. A use for the form of contradiction.) A main characteristic that distinguishes imagination from sensory impression and from hallucination is this, that the one who imagines does not relate to the imagination in an observing manner, i.e., that the imagination is arbitrary.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#395.4) <span class="fac">[395\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/395.webp)</span> {#ts-229-395-4}

<span class="series-number">1554</span> Imagine a conversation, one of whose partners is yourself, so that you yourself speak in the imagination. What you yourself say, you will probably feel in your body (larynx, chest). But that only describes, does not define, the activity of speaking in the imagination.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#396.1) <span class="fac">[396\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/396.webp)</span> {#ts-229-396-1}

<span class="series-number">1555</span> The feeling of uncanniness. How does it manifest itself? The _duration_ of such a ‘feeling’. What does an interruption of the feeling look like? Would it be possible, for example, to alternate having it for one second, and not having it? Is not one of its characteristics also a characteristic type of course (beginning and end), which distinguishes it, for example, from a sensory perception?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#396.2) <span class="fac">[396\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/396.webp)</span> {#ts-229-396-2}

<span class="series-number">1556</span> Speaking the music. Don't forget that a poem, even if written in the language of communication, is not used in the language-game of communication. Couldn't one imagine that someone who has never known music and comes to us and hears someone play a thoughtful Chopin piece would be convinced that this is a language and that one is only keeping the sense secret from him. In word-language, there is a strong musical element. (A sigh, the tone of voice of the question, the announcement, the longing, all the countless _gestures_ of the tone.)


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#396.3) <span class="fac">[396\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/396.webp)</span> {#ts-229-396-3}

<span class="series-number">1557</span> “One should seek nothing behind the phenomena; they themselves are the teaching.” (Goethe.)


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#396.4+397.1) <span class="fac">[396\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/396.webp),[397\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/397.webp)</span> {#ts-229-396-4--397-1}

<span class="series-number">1558</span> I observe his face closely. Why? What does it teach me? Whether he is sad or happy, for example. But why does that interest me? Well, if I get to know his mood, it's as if I were getting to know the state of a body (its temperature, for example); I can draw all sorts of conclusions from that. And that is why, in the same case, I do not observe my own face. If I observed myself, then my face would no longer be a reliable index; and I could not draw any conclusions from it, even if it were for another person.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#397.2) <span class="fac">[397\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/397.webp)</span> {#ts-229-397-2}

<span class="series-number">1559</span> Being ashamed of a thought. Is one ashamed of having spoken this or that sentence to oneself in one's imagination? Language simply has multiple roots; it has roots, not _one_ root.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#397.3) <span class="fac">[397\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/397.webp)</span> {#ts-229-397-3}

<span class="series-number">1560</span> “The thought stood before my mind at this moment.” – And how? – “I had this image.” – So was the image the thought? No, because if I had only communicated the image to someone, he would not have received the thought.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#397.4) <span class="fac">[397\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/397.webp)</span> {#ts-229-397-4}

<span class="series-number">1561</span> The image was the key. Or it _appeared_ as the key.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#397.5+398.1) <span class="fac">[397\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/397.webp),[398\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/398.webp)</span> {#ts-229-397-5--398-1}

<span class="series-number">1562</span> How do visual impressions differ from auditory impressions? – Should I answer: “It cannot be said; but whoever sees and hears knows that they are _totally_ different”? Could one imagine that in a person _one_ particular visual impression would be the same as _one_ particular auditory impression? so that he could receive this one impression through the eye and through the ear? Would he perhaps point to a picture and strike a note on the piano and tell us that _these_ two are identical? And would we believe him? And why not? Would we believe him that the ‘affection of the mind’ is the same in both cases? And if we believed it, how could we use this fact?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#398.2) <span class="fac">[398\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/398.webp)</span> {#ts-229-398-2}

<span class="series-number">1563</span> The family tree of psychological phenomena: I am not striving for _exactness_, but for surveyability.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#398.3+399.1) <span class="fac">[398\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/398.webp),[399\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/399.webp)</span> {#ts-229-398-3--399-1}

<span class="series-number">1564</span> What holds the bundle of ‘sensory impressions’ together are their relations to one another. What is ‘red’ is also ‘sweet’ and ‘hard’ and ‘cold’, and ‘sounds’ when struck. In the language-game with these words, it does not originally mean “This _seems_ red,” but “This _is_ red” (hard, etc.). Our agreement is essential to the language-game. But it is different with “pleasant,” “unpleasant,” “beautiful,” “ugly.” Pain is in some ways analogous to the other sensory impressions, in some ways different. There is a facial expression, exclamations, gestures of pain (as of joy), signs of _rejection_, a reception that is characteristic of pain, but not one that is characteristic of the color red. Bitterness is related to pain in this respect. One could imagine a pressure without a sensory organ. One could imagine someone hearing and, as a result, learning almost all the language-games with the words for auditory impressions, without having ears, and without one knowing ‘_what_’ he hears with. The fact that one hears with the ears is only shown in a relatively small number of cases. Yes, it could be that someone hears as we all do, and only later one realizes that his _ears_ are deaf. The _content_ of the experiences. One would like to say “_Thus_ I see red,” “_Thus_ I hear the sound that you strike,” “_Thus_ I feel pleasure,” “_Thus_ I experience sorrow,” or also “_That_ is what one experiences when one is sad; _that_, when one is happy,” etc. One would like to populate a world, analogous to the physical one, with these _Thus_ and _That_. But this only makes sense where there is a picture of the _experienced_ thing, to which one can point in these _statements_.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#399.2) <span class="fac">[399\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/399.webp)</span> {#ts-229-399-2}

<span class="series-number">1565</span> If only _one person_ had ever made a bodily movement, – could the question be whether it was voluntary or involuntary?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#399.3) <span class="fac">[399\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/399.webp)</span> {#ts-229-399-3}

<span class="series-number">1566</span> “When I exert myself, I _do_ something; I don't just have a sensation.” And that is the case; for one tells someone: “Exert yourself!” and he can express the intention “I will now exert myself.” And when he says “I can't anymore!” – that does not mean “I can no longer endure the feeling in my limbs – the pain, for example.” – On the other hand, one _suffers_ from the exertion, as from pain. “I am completely exhausted” – if someone said that, but moved as briskly as ever, one would not understand him.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#400.1) <span class="fac">[400\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/400.webp)</span> {#ts-229-400-1}

<span class="series-number">1567</span> The aspect is subject to the will. I cannot see something as red if it appears blue to me, and it makes no sense to say “See this as red,” but rather “See this as ‥‥.” And that the aspect (at least to a certain extent) is arbitrary seems to be essential to it, as is the idea that it is. I mean: the arbitrariness does not seem to me (but why?) to be merely an addition; as if one said, “This movement can, as a matter of experience, also be brought about _in this way_.” That is: it is essential that one can say “See it now _in this way_!” and “Imagine ‥‥!” For that is connected with the fact that the aspect tells us nothing about the ‘external world’. One can teach the words “red” and “blue” by saying “This is red and not blue”; but one cannot teach someone the meaning of “figure” and “ground” by pointing to a figure that is ambiguous.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#400.2) <span class="fac">[400\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/400.webp)</span> {#ts-229-400-2}

<span class="series-number">1568</span> We do not learn to know images and only later learn to guide them with our will. And, of course, it is completely false to think that we guide them, as it were, by means of our will. As if the will governed them, as commands might govern people. As if the will were an influence, a force, or also: a primary _action_, which is then the cause of the perceptible external actions.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#400.3+401.1) <span class="fac">[400\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/400.webp),[401\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/401.webp)</span> {#ts-229-400-3--401-1}

<span class="series-number">1569</span> Is it correct to say: what makes an action voluntary are the psychological phenomena in which it is embedded? (The psychological environment.) Are, for example, my normal walking movements “voluntary” in a _non-potential_ sense?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#401.2) <span class="fac">[401\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/401.webp)</span> {#ts-229-401-2}

<span class="series-number">1570</span> A child stamps its feet in anger: is this not voluntary? And do I know anything about its movement sensations when it does this? _Stamping in anger is voluntary._ Coming when one is called, in ordinary circumstances, is voluntary. Involuntary walking, strolling, eating, speaking, singing would be (a) walking, eating, speaking, etc. in abnormal circumstances. For example, _unconscious_: when one acts as in a trance; or when the movement takes place, and one knows nothing about it as soon as one closes one's eyes; or when one cannot stop the movement, no matter how hard one tries; etc.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#401.3+402.1) <span class="fac">[401\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/401.webp),[402\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/402.webp)</span> {#ts-229-401-3--402-1}

<span class="series-number">1571</span> No assumption seems more natural to me than that associating, or thinking, has no process in the brain associated with it; so that it would be impossible to infer thinking processes from brain processes. I mean this: when I speak, or write, I assume that there is a system of impulses emanating from my brain that is associated with my spoken or written thought. But why should this _system_ continue further in a central direction? Why shouldn't this order, as it were, emerge from chaos? The case would be similar to that of – certain species of plants reproducing by seeds, so that a seed always produces the same species of plant from which it was produced, – but that _nothing_ in the seed of the plant that grows from it corresponds to it; so that it is impossible to infer the properties, or structure of the plant that grows from it from the properties, or structure of the seed – that one can only do this from its _history_. Thus, from something completely amorphous, an organism could, as it were, come into being without a cause; and there is no reason why this should not also be the case with our thought, i.e. with our speaking or writing etc. _actually_.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#402.2) <span class="fac">[402\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/402.webp)</span> {#ts-229-402-2}

<span class="series-number">1572</span> It is therefore quite possible that certain psychological phenomena cannot be investigated physiologically _because_ nothing corresponds to them physiologically.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#402.3) <span class="fac">[402\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/402.webp)</span> {#ts-229-402-3}

<span class="series-number">1573</span> I saw this man years ago; now I see him again, recognize him, remember his name. And why must there now be a cause for this remembering in my nervous system? Why must something, whatever it may be, _in some form_ have been stored there? Why _must_ he have left a trace? Why shouldn't there be a psychological regularity to which _no_ physiological one corresponds? If this upsets our concepts of causality, then it is time for them to be upset.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#402.4+403.1) <span class="fac">[402\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/402.webp),[403\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/403.webp)</span> {#ts-229-402-4--403-1}

<span class="series-number">1574</span> The prejudice for psycho-physical parallelism is also a fruit of the primitive conception of grammar. For if one allows for causality between psychological phenomena that is not physiologically mediated, one is, in effect, admitting a nebulous mental essence.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#403.2) <span class="fac">[403\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/403.webp)</span> {#ts-229-403-2}

<span class="series-number">1575</span> Must the verb “I believe” have a past tense? Well, if instead of “I believe he is coming” we always said “He probably is coming” (or something similar), but still said “I used to believe…” – then the verb “to believe” would have no _present_. It is characteristic of the way we are accustomed to look at language that we believe that in the end there must be uniformity, symmetry; instead of, conversely, assuming that it _cannot_ exist.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#403.3+404.1) <span class="fac">[403\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/403.webp),[404\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/404.webp)</span> {#ts-229-403-3--404-1}

<span class="series-number">1576</span> Imagine this phenomenon: If I want someone to remember a text that I read aloud to them, so that they can repeat it to me later, I must give them a piece of paper and a pencil; and while I speak, they write lines, signs on the paper; if they are to reproduce the text later, they follow those lines with their eyes and say the text. But I assume that their record is not _writing_; it is not connected to the words of the text by rules; and yet they cannot reproduce the text without this record; and if something is changed in it, or if it is partially destroyed, they get stuck in ‘reading’, or speak the text uncertainly, or unreliably, or cannot find the words at all. – But that could be conceived! – What I called the ‘record’ would then not be a _reproduction_ of the text, not a translation into another symbolism. The text would not be _laid down_ in the record. And why should it be laid down in our nervous system?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#404.2) <span class="fac">[404\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/404.webp)</span> {#ts-229-404-2}

<span class="series-number">1576</span> Why shouldn’t a law of nature connect the initial and final states of a system, while allowing the state in between to pass by? (But let us not think of _effect_!)


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#404.3) <span class="fac">[404\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/404.webp)</span> {#ts-229-404-3}

<span class="series-number">1577</span> What one calls a change in thinking is, of course, not only a change in speaking, but also a change in doing.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#404.4) <span class="fac">[404\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/404.webp)</span> {#ts-229-404-4}

<span class="series-number">1578</span> One sees the terminology, but not the _technique_ of its application.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#404.5) <span class="fac">[404\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/404.webp)</span> {#ts-229-404-5}

<span class="series-number">1579</span> One says “He seems to be in terrible pain,” even if one has no doubt that the appearance is not deceptive. Why doesn’t one say “I seem to be in terrible pain,” because this, at least, ought to make _sense_? I could say that during a rehearsal; and also “I seem to have the _intention_ to ‥‥,” etc. Everyone will say: “Of course, I don’t say that, because I _know_ whether I am in pain.” It usually doesn’t _interest_ me whether I seem to be in pain; because the conclusions that I draw from this _impression_ in the other, I do not draw _for myself_. I don’t say: “I am groaning terribly, I must go to the doctor”; but rather “He is groaning terribly, he must ‥‥”.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#404.6+405.1) <span class="fac">[404\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/404.webp),[405\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/405.webp)</span> {#ts-229-404-6--405-1}

<span class="series-number">1580</span> If this makes no _sense_: “I know that I am in pain” – or this: “I feel my pain” – then it also makes no _sense_ to say: “I don’t care about my own groaning, because _I know_ that I am in pain” – or “because I _feel_ my pain.” But it is true: “I don’t care about my groaning.”


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#405.2) <span class="fac">[405\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/405.webp)</span> {#ts-229-405-2}

<span class="series-number">1581</span> I conclude from the observation of his _behaviour_ that he must go to the doctor; but I do not draw this conclusion for myself from the observation of my own _behaviour_. Or rather: I do sometimes do this, but _not_ in analogous cases.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#405.3) <span class="fac">[405\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/405.webp)</span> {#ts-229-405-3}

<span class="series-number">1582</span> It helps here if one considers that it is a primitive _behaviour_ to care for the painful spot of the other, to treat it, and not only one’s own – i.e. to pay attention to the other’s _pain-behaviour_, as well as not to pay attention to one’s own _pain-behaviour_.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#405.4) <span class="fac">[405\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/405.webp)</span> {#ts-229-405-4}

<span class="series-number">1583</span> But what does the word “primitive” mean here? Well, that the way of _behaving_ is _pre-linguistic_: that a language-game is _based_ on it, that it is the prototype of a way of thinking and not the result of thinking.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#405.5) <span class="fac">[405\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/405.webp)</span> {#ts-229-405-5}

<span class="series-number">1584</span> One can say that an _explanation_ is “incorrectly harnessed” in the following way: we cared for the other because, by analogy with our own case, we believed that he also had a painful _experience_. – Instead of saying: Learn from this particular chapter of our _conduct_ – from this language-game – what function “analogy” and “belief” have _in it_.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#405.6+406.1) <span class="fac">[405\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/405.webp),[406\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/406.webp)</span> {#ts-229-405-6--406-1}

<span class="series-number">1585</span> “How is it that I see the tree upright, even when I tilt my head to the side, and thus the image on my retina is of a slanted tree?” How is it, then, that I still call the tree upright under these circumstances? – “Well, I am conscious of the tilt of my head, and therefore make the necessary correction to my perception of my visual impressions.” – But doesn’t this mean confusing the primary with the secondary? Imagine that we knew _absolutely_ _nothing_ about the internal structure of the eye – would this problem even _arise_? We are not actually making any corrections _here_; this is merely an explanation. Indeed – but now that the structure of the eye is known, – how is it that we act this way, that we react this way? But must there be a physiological explanation here? How, if we were to rely on it? – But that is not how you would speak if you were testing the behaviour of a machine! – Well, who says that in this sense the living being, the animal body, is a machine? –


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#406.2) <span class="fac">[406\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/406.webp)</span> {#ts-229-406-2}

<span class="series-number">1586</span> One can notice a change in a face and describe it in words, saying that the face has taken on a harder expression, – and yet be unable to describe the change in spatial terms. This is immensely important. – Perhaps someone will say: when one does this, one is not describing the change in the face, but only the effect on oneself; but why shouldn't a description in terms of shape and color also be _this_?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#406.3) <span class="fac">[406\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/406.webp)</span> {#ts-229-406-3}

<span class="series-number">1587</span> One can also say “He made _that_ face” or “His face changed _like this_,” by imitating it – and still be unable to describe the change in any other way. ((There are, after all, far more language-games than Carnap and others can imagine.))


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#406.4) <span class="fac">[406\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/406.webp)</span> {#ts-229-406-4}

<span class="series-number">1588</span> Being conscious that ‥‥ can disturb me in my work; knowing does not.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#406.5) <span class="fac">[406\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/406.webp)</span> {#ts-229-406-5}

<span class="series-number">1589</span> How do I know that a dog hears something constantly, constantly receives visual impressions, experiences joy, fear, pain? What do I know about the ‘content of experience’ of a dog?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#407.1) <span class="fac">[407\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/407.webp)</span> {#ts-229-407-1}

<span class="series-number">1590</span> Are colors really siblings? Are they only different in color, and not also in kind? Are sight, hearing, and taste really siblings? Don't just look for similarities in order to justify a concept, but also for connections. The father passes on his name to his son, even if the latter is very different from him.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#407.2) <span class="fac">[407\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/407.webp)</span> {#ts-229-407-2}

<span class="series-number">1591</span> Compare a terrible fright and a sudden, sharp pain. It is the sensation of pain that is terrible – but is it the sensation of fright? If someone collapses in my presence – is that just the cause of a highly unpleasant momentary sensation _in me_? And how can this question be answered? Does the person who reports the terrible event complain about the sensations, the stopping of breath, etc.? If one wants to help someone get over the fright – does one treat the body? Isn’t one more likely to calm the frightened person by talking about the event, the cause?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#407.3) <span class="fac">[407\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/407.webp)</span> {#ts-229-407-3}

<span class="series-number">1592</span> Someone who pretends to be sad in his study will certainly easily become conscious of the tensions in his face. But really grieve, or follow a sad scene in a film, and ask yourself whether you were conscious of your face.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#407.4) <span class="fac">[407\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/407.webp)</span> {#ts-229-407-4}

<span class="series-number">1593</span> A connection between the moods in sensory impressions is that we use mood terms to describe sensory impressions and images. We say that a theme, a landscape, is sad, cheerful, etc. But much more important is, of course, that we describe the human face, the action, the behaviour, through all the mood terms.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#407.5+408.1) <span class="fac">[407\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/407.webp),[408\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/408.webp)</span> {#ts-229-407-5--408-1}

<span class="series-number">1594</span> Consciousness in the other face. Look at the face of the other and see the consciousness in it and a certain **tone** of consciousness. You see in it, in him, joy, indifference, interest, emotion, dullness, etc. The light in the face of the other. Do you look in _yourself_ to recognize the anger in _his_ face? It is there just as clearly as in your own breast. (And what does one want to say now? That the face of the other stimulates me to imitate, & that I therefore feel small movements & muscle tensions in myself, & that the sum of these is _mine_? Nonsense. Nonsense, – for you are making assumptions instead of merely describing. Whoever has explanations swirling around in his head neglects to focus on the most important facts.)


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#408.2) <span class="fac">[408\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/408.webp)</span> {#ts-229-408-2}

<span class="series-number">1595</span> Knowledge and having an opinion do not have a facial expression. There is probably a tone, a gesture of conviction, but only if something is said in this tone, with this gesture.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#408.3) <span class="fac">[408\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/408.webp)</span> {#ts-229-408-3}

<span class="series-number">1596</span> “Consciousness is so clearly present in their face and behaviour, as it is in myself.”


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#408.4) <span class="fac">[408\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/408.webp)</span> {#ts-229-408-4}

<span class="series-number">1597</span> What would it mean to be mistaken in the fact that they have a mind, consciousness? and what would it mean that I am mistaken and have none myself? What would it mean to say “I am not conscious.” – – But don't I still know that consciousness is in me? – So I know it, and yet the statement that it is so has no purpose? And how remarkable that one can learn to communicate about this matter with other people!


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#408.5) <span class="fac">[408\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/408.webp)</span> {#ts-229-408-5}

<span class="series-number">1598</span> One can pretend to be unconscious; but also conscious?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#408.6+409.1) <span class="fac">[408\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/408.webp),[409\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/409.webp)</span> {#ts-229-408-6--409-1}

<span class="series-number">1599</span> What if someone seriously told me that they really don't know whether they are dreaming or awake? – Can this situation exist: someone says “I think I am dreaming now”; they soon wake up afterwards, remember that utterance in the dream, and say “So I was really right!” – – This narrative can only mean: someone dreamed that they said they were dreaming. Imagine, someone unconscious says (perhaps during anesthesia) “I am in consciousness” – would we say “They must know”? And if someone speaks in their sleep, saying “I am sleeping” – would we say “They are quite right”? Does someone tell an untruth when they say to me: “I am not conscious”? (And what about the truth, if they say it while unconscious? And what if a parrot said “I don't understand a word” or a gramophone said “I am just a machine”?)


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#409.2) <span class="fac">[409\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/409.webp)</span> {#ts-229-409-2}

<span class="series-number">1600</span> Imagine, in a daydream, I have myself say “I am just fantasizing”; would that be true? Imagine, I write such a fantasy, or narrative, a fantasized dialogue, and in it I say “I am fantasizing” – – but when I write it down, – how does it show that these words are words of fantasy and that I have not stepped out of the fantasy? Wouldn't it really be possible that the dreamer, as it were, stepping out of the dream, speaks in their sleep, saying “I am dreaming”? It would probably be conceivable that such a language-game existed. This is connected with the problem of ‘meaning’. Because in a dialogue, I can write “I am healthy” and not mean it, even though it is true. The words belong to this language-game and not to that one.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#409.3) <span class="fac">[409\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/409.webp)</span> {#ts-229-409-3}

<span class="series-number">1601</span> ‘True’ and ‘False’ in a dream. I dream that it is raining and that I say “It is raining” – – on the other hand: I dream that I say “I am dreaming”.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#409.4) <span class="fac">[409\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/409.webp)</span> {#ts-229-409-4}

<span class="series-number">1602</span> Does the verb “to dream” have a present tense? How does one learn to use this?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#409.5) <span class="fac">[409\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/409.webp)</span> {#ts-229-409-5}

<span class="series-number">1603</span> A language-game analogous to a fragment of another. A space projected into limited pieces of a space.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#409.6+410.1) <span class="fac">[409\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/409.webp),[410\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/410.webp)</span> {#ts-229-409-6--410-1}

<span class="series-number">1604</span> Suppose I had an experience, similar to waking up, and found myself in a completely different environment, with people assuring me that I had been asleep. Suppose further that I insist that I did not dream, but in some way lived outside my sleeping body. What is the function of this assertion?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#410.2) <span class="fac">[410\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/410.webp)</span> {#ts-229-410-2}

<span class="series-number">1605</span> “‘I am conscious,’ this is a statement for which no doubt is possible.” Why shouldn’t this say the same thing as this: “‘I am conscious’ is not a sentence”? One could also say: What is the harm in someone saying that “I am conscious” is a statement that allows no doubt? How do I contradict him? Suppose someone said this to me – why shouldn’t I get used to not answering him, instead of perhaps starting an argument? Why shouldn’t I treat his words as I would his whistling or humming?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#410.3) <span class="fac">[410\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/410.webp)</span> {#ts-229-410-3}

<span class="series-number">1606</span> “Nothing is as certain as that I am conscious.” Why then shouldn’t I just leave it at that? This certainty, like a great force whose point of application does not move, which therefore does no work.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#410.4) <span class="fac">[410\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/410.webp)</span> {#ts-229-410-4}

<span class="series-number">1607</span> In a game of dice, someone rolls a 5, then a 4, and says “If only I had rolled a 4 instead of the 5, I would have won!” The condition is not physical, but only mathematical, for one could reply: “If you had rolled a 4 first, who knows what you would have rolled afterward!”


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#410.5) <span class="fac">[410\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/410.webp)</span> {#ts-229-410-5}

<span class="series-number">1608</span> If you now say “The use of the subjunctive is based on the belief in a law of nature” – then one can reply: “It is not based on this belief; it and this belief are on the same level.”


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#410.6) <span class="fac">[410\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/410.webp)</span> {#ts-229-410-6}

<span class="series-number">1609</span> Fate is in contrast to the law of nature. One wants to understand the law of nature and use it; one does not want to understand fate.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#411.1) <span class="fac">[411\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/411.webp)</span> {#ts-229-411-1}

<span class="series-number">1610</span> The concept of the ‘fragment’. It is not easy to describe the use of this word, even in a casual way.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#411.2) <span class="fac">[411\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/411.webp)</span> {#ts-229-411-2}

<span class="series-number">1611</span> If we want to describe the use of a word, is it not similar to wanting to paint a portrait of a face? I see it clearly; the expression of these features is well known to me; and if I were to paint it, I would not know where to begin. And if I actually make a picture, it is completely inadequate. If I had a description in front of me, I would recognize it; I might also notice errors in it. But the fact that I can do that does not mean that I could have given the description myself.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#411.3) <span class="fac">[411\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/411.webp)</span> {#ts-229-411-3}

<span class="series-number">1612</span> Two objects ‘belong together’. One teaches a child to ‘arrange’ things, and accompanies the activity with the words “These belong together”. The child also learns this expression. The child could also arrange the things with the help of these words and certain gestures. But the words can also be merely an accompaniment to the action. A language-game. Imagine such a game without words, but with the accompaniment of music that is appropriate to the actions.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#411.4) <span class="fac">[411\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/411.webp)</span> {#ts-229-411-4}

<span class="series-number">1613</span> “Put it _here_” – indicating the place with my finger – this is an _absolute_ indication of location. And, whoever says that the space is absolute would like to say as an argument for this: “There is indeed a _place: here_.”


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#411.5) <span class="fac">[411\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/411.webp)</span> {#ts-229-411-5}

<span class="series-number">1614</span> ‘Experiencing similarity’. Think of the language-game: “Recognizing similarities,” or “Indicating similarities,” or “Arranging things according to their similarities.” Where is the special experience here? The special content of the experience that one is looking for?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#412.1) <span class="fac">[412\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/412.webp)</span> {#ts-229-412-1}

<span class="series-number">1615</span> The duration of sensation. Compare the duration of a tonal sensation with the duration of the tactile sensation that teaches you that you are holding a ball in your hand; and with the “feeling” that teaches you that your knees are bent. And here we have another reason why we might say that the sensation of posture has no content.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#412.2) <span class="fac">[412\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/412.webp)</span> {#ts-229-412-2}

<span class="series-number">1616</span> Philosophical Investigations: conceptual investigations. The essence of metaphysics: that it does not have a clear distinction between factual and conceptual investigations. The metaphysical question is always, in appearance, a factual one, even though the problem is a conceptual one.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#412.3) <span class="fac">[412\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/412.webp)</span> {#ts-229-412-3}

<span class="series-number">1617</span> What does a conceptual investigation do? Is it one of the natural histories of human concepts? Well, natural history describes, say, plants and animals. But could it not be that plants have been described in every detail, and now someone comes along who sees analogies in their structure that one had not seen before? That he thus creates a new order in these descriptions. He says, for example: “Do not compare this part with this; but rather with that!” (Goethe wanted to do something like this.) And in doing so, he does not necessarily speak of _descent_; nevertheless, the new arrangement could also give scientific investigation a new direction. He says “Look at it _this_ way!” – and this can have various advantages and consequences.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#412.4) <span class="fac">[412\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/412.webp)</span> {#ts-229-412-4}

<span class="series-number">1618</span> Why do we count? Has it proven practical? Do we have our concepts, e.g., the psychological ones, because it is advantageous? – And yet we have _certain_ concepts precisely for this reason, we have introduced them for this reason.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#413.1) <span class="fac">[413\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/413.webp)</span> {#ts-229-413-1}

<span class="series-number">1619</span> One should not think that it is a simplification to consider seeing with one eye instead of seeing with both eyes; if one is clear that one does not feel seeing in the eyes. The idea of the visual object is much more difficult to carry out for two-eyed seeing. Because what is the two-eyed ‘visual image’? ‘The portrait of what one really _sees_’ ‘the visual impression itself’.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#413.2) <span class="fac">[413\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/413.webp)</span> {#ts-229-413-2}

<span class="series-number">1620</span> It seems to one: if I only had the right colors and things at my disposal, I could _exactly_ represent what I see. And in a way, this is true to a certain extent. And that report of what I have in front of me, and the description of what I see, have the same form. – But they _leave_ out, for example, the wandering of the gaze. But also, for example, the reading of a script in the field of vision and every aspect of what is seen.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#413.3) <span class="fac">[413\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/413.webp)</span> {#ts-229-413-3}

<span class="series-number">1621</span> If what you are looking at is a large panel, or a flat wall with a figure on it, then a picture of this figure can be considered an accurate description. If the figure is, for example, F, what more can one want than that it be accurately drawn; and yet there is still a quite different description that is not contained in the drawing. So also, if the figure is a face.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#413.4) <span class="fac">[413\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/413.webp)</span> {#ts-229-413-4}

<span class="series-number">1622</span> What is, in _one_ sense, a slight inaccuracy in the description, is, in another sense, a large one.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#413.5) <span class="fac">[413\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/413.webp)</span> {#ts-229-413-5}

<span class="series-number">1623</span> Active and passive. Can one command it, or not? This may seem like a far-fetched distinction, but it is not. It is similar to: “Can one (_logical_ possibility) decide to do it, or not?” – And that means: how is it surrounded by thoughts, feelings, etc.?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#413.6+414.1) <span class="fac">[413\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/413.webp),[414\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/414.webp)</span> {#ts-229-413-6--414-1}

<span class="series-number">1624</span> What would a society of all deaf people look like? What, a society of ‘feebleminded’ people? _Important question_! How, then, is a society that has never played many of our usual language-games?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#414.2) <span class="fac">[414\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/414.webp)</span> {#ts-229-414-2}

<span class="series-number">1625</span> To be conscious of a sameness of colors in a picture, or that _this_ color is darker than that one. Am I conscious all the time, while listening to this piece, that it is by ‥‥? When is one conscious of a fact _consciously_?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#414.3) <span class="fac">[414\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/414.webp)</span> {#ts-229-414-3}

<span class="series-number">1626</span> Love is not a feeling. Love is tested, pain is not.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#414.4) <span class="fac">[414\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/414.webp)</span> {#ts-229-414-4}

<span class="series-number">1627</span> I see something _in various contexts_. (Is this more related to imagining than to seeing?)


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#414.5) <span class="fac">[414\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/414.webp)</span> {#ts-229-414-5}

<span class="series-number">1628</span> It is as if one had applied a concept to what one sees, and now sees it along with that concept. This concept, which is hardly visible in itself, nevertheless spreads an _ordering veil_ over the objects.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#414.6) <span class="fac">[414\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/414.webp)</span> {#ts-229-414-6}

<span class="series-number">1629</span> “What do you see?” (language-game) – – “What do you _really_ see?”


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#414.7) <span class="fac">[414\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/414.webp)</span> {#ts-229-414-7}

<span class="series-number">1630</span> Let us imagine seeing as something mysterious, without any physiological explanation.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#414.8) <span class="fac">[414\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/414.webp)</span> {#ts-229-414-8}

<span class="series-number">1631</span> To the question “What do you see?”, various descriptions are given as answers. – If someone now says: “I see the aspect, the organization, just as well as the shapes and colors” – what is meant by this? That one includes all of this in ‘seeing’? or that there is a great similarity here? – And what can I say about that? I can point out similarities and dissimilarities.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#414.9+415.1) <span class="fac">[414\[9\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/414.webp),[415\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/415.webp)</span> {#ts-229-414-9--415-1}

<span class="series-number">1632</span> Could one not consider it madness if a person recognizes a drawing as a portrait of N.N. and exclaims, “That is Mr. N.N.!” – “He must be crazy,” one says of him, “He sees a piece of paper with black lines on it and thinks it is a person!”


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#415.2) <span class="fac">[415\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/415.webp)</span> {#ts-229-415-2}

<span class="series-number">1633</span> ‘Seeing the figure _as_ ‥‥’ has something occult, something incomprehensible about it. One would like to say: “Something has changed and nothing has changed.” – – But do not try to explain it! Rather, consider the rest of seeing as occult as well.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#415.3) <span class="fac">[415\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/415.webp)</span> {#ts-229-415-3}

<span class="series-number">1634</span> The expression of that experience is and remains: “I see it as a mountain,” “I see it as a wedge,” “I see it with this base and this point, but overturned,” etc. And the words “mountain,” “wedge,” “base,” “overturned” are also just strokes or sounds – _with a use_.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#415.4) <span class="fac">[415\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/415.webp)</span> {#ts-229-415-4}

<span class="series-number">1635</span> Think of a depiction of a face from the front and in profile at the same time, as in some modern pictures. A depiction in which a movement, a change, a shifting of the gaze is included. Does such a picture not _actually_ depict what one sees?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#415.5) <span class="fac">[415\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/415.webp)</span> {#ts-229-415-5}

<span class="series-number">1636</span> “I forgive you.” Can one say “I am busy forgiving you”? No. But that does not mean that there is not a process that one could also call “forgiving” – but does not call it that – I mean, the resolving of the inner conflict that can lead to forgiving.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#415.6) <span class="fac">[415\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/415.webp)</span> {#ts-229-415-6}

<span class="series-number">1637</span> I would like to say: there are aspects that are _mainly_ determined by thoughts and associations, and others that are ‘purely visual’ and occur automatically and change, almost like afterimages.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#415.7) <span class="fac">[415\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/415.webp)</span> {#ts-229-415-7}

<span class="series-number">1638</span> What Köhler does not address is the fact that one can look at the figure 🖵 one way or another, that the aspect, at least to a certain extent, is subject to the will.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#416.1) <span class="fac">[416\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/416.webp)</span> {#ts-229-416-1}

<span class="series-number">1639</span> I can pay attention to the course of my pain; but not in the same way to the course of my belief or knowledge.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#416.2) <span class="fac">[416\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/416.webp)</span> {#ts-229-416-2}

<span class="series-number">1640</span> The observation of duration can be uninterrupted or interrupted. How do you observe your knowledge, your opinions? and on the other hand, an afterimage, a pain? Is there an uninterrupted observation of my ability to perform the multiplication ‥‥?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#416.3) <span class="fac">[416\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/416.webp)</span> {#ts-229-416-3}

<span class="series-number">1641</span> [→ ))] One _could_ explain this by saying that the aspect is related to the movement of the eyes.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#416.4) <span class="fac">[416\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/416.webp)</span> {#ts-229-416-4}

<span class="series-number">1642</span> Analogy to the contrast between ‘value’ and ‘limit value’ of a function. ((important))


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#416.5) <span class="fac">[416\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/416.webp)</span> {#ts-229-416-5}

<span class="series-number">1643</span> That the aspect is subject to the will is not a fact that does not touch its very essence. For what if we could see things arbitrarily as red or green? How would one then learn to use the words “red” and “green”? There would then be, above all, not a ‘red object,’ at most one that one sees more easily as red than as green.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#416.6+417.1) <span class="fac">[416\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/416.webp),[417\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/417.webp)</span> {#ts-229-416-6--417-1}

<span class="series-number">1644</span> Isn't what Köhler says approximately: “One could not consider something to be this or that if one could not _see_ it as this or that”? Does a child begin by seeing something in a certain way before learning to consider it as this or that? Does it first learn to answer the question “How do you see this?” and only then “What _is_ this?” –


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#417.2) <span class="fac">[417\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/417.webp)</span> {#ts-229-417-2}

<span class="series-number">1645</span> Can one say that one must be able to visually grasp the armchair as a whole in order to recognize it as a thing? – Do I visually grasp that armchair as a thing, and which of my reactions show that? Which reactions of a person show that he recognizes something as a thing, and which show that he _sees_ something as a whole, as a thing?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#417.3) <span class="fac">[417\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/417.webp)</span> {#ts-229-417-3}

<span class="series-number">1646</span> One could imagine it as follows: One examines in what way a child depicts plane figures, if one has not taught it any type of depiction, and if it has never seen spatial objects.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#417.4) <span class="fac">[417\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/417.webp)</span> {#ts-229-417-4}

<span class="series-number">1647</span> I learn to describe what I see; and in doing so, I learn all kinds of language-games. –


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#417.5) <span class="fac">[417\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/417.webp)</span> {#ts-229-417-5}

<span class="series-number">1648</span> Not “How can I describe what I see?” – but: “What does one _call_ ‘a description of what one sees’?” And the answer to _this_ question is: Very different things.”


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#417.6+418.1) <span class="fac">[417\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/417.webp),[418\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/418.webp)</span> {#ts-229-417-6--418-1}

<span class="series-number">1649</span> Köhler says that only very few people spontaneously see the figure 4 in the drawing 🖵 and this is certainly true. How does a person differ from normal people, who in his description of plane figures, or when he copies them, deviates radically from the norm in that he uses different ‘units’ when copying and describing? I.e., how will this person also differ from normal people in other things?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#418.2) <span class="fac">[418\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/418.webp)</span> {#ts-229-418-2}

<span class="series-number">1650</span> A person could have a high talent for drawing, I mean the talent to draw objects, a room, for example, very accurately, and could still repeatedly make small errors against the _sense_; so that one could say “He does not grasp an object as an object.” He would, for example, never make a mistake, such as the painter Klecksel, who paints two eyes in profile. His _knowledge_ would mislead him.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#418.3) <span class="fac">[418\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/418.webp)</span> {#ts-229-418-3}

<span class="series-number">1651</span> The seductive concept is: “the _complete_ description of what one sees.”


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#418.4) <span class="fac">[418\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/418.webp)</span> {#ts-229-418-4}

<span class="series-number">1652</span> Always eliminate the private object by assuming: it changes constantly; but you don't notice it because your memory constantly deceives you.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#418.5) <span class="fac">[418\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/418.webp)</span> {#ts-229-418-5}

<span class="series-number">1653</span> “Whoever sees something, sees something specific” – but that doesn't mean anything. It is as if one wanted to say: If no depiction resembles the visual impression, it does resemble itself.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#418.6) <span class="fac">[418\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/418.webp)</span> {#ts-229-418-6}

<span class="series-number">1654</span> It could be that someone, when asked “What do you see here?” correctly redraws the figure, but answers “No” to the question “Do you see a 4”, even though he himself formed it when redrawing.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#419.1) <span class="fac">[419\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/419.webp)</span> {#ts-229-419-1}

<span class="series-number">1655</span> What do I communicate to the person to whom I make the communication that I now see the ornament _like this_? (Strange question.) – That is, “In which language-game is this sentence used?” – “What do we do with this sentence?”


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#419.2) <span class="fac">[419\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/419.webp)</span> {#ts-229-419-2}

<span class="series-number">1656</span> Let us assume that certain aspects can be explained by eye movement: Then one would like to say that these are purely optical in nature; and there must therefore be a description of them that does not have to make use of analogies from other fields. Then one must be able to replace the command “See this as ‥‥!” with: “Let the gaze wander so and so,” or something similar.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#419.3) <span class="fac">[419\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/419.webp)</span> {#ts-229-419-3}

<span class="series-number">1657</span> But it is not true that an experience that can be demonstrably related to eye movement can be generated by it, and therefore can be described by a sequence of visual images. (About as much as the person who imagines a tone also imagines a sequence of air vibrations.)


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#419.4) <span class="fac">[419\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/419.webp)</span> {#ts-229-419-4}

<span class="series-number">1658</span> Hold a drawing of a face upside down, & you cannot recognize the _expression_ of the face. Perhaps you can also see that it is laughing, but not exactly _how_ it is laughing. You could not imitate the laugh, or describe its character more precisely. And yet, the inverted image can represent the object very accurately.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#420.1) <span class="fac">[420\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/420.webp)</span> {#ts-229-420-1}

<span class="series-number">1659</span> One must bear in mind that seeing something in a _certain way_ can have a similar effect to changing what is seen, e.g., by using parentheses, underlining, summarizing it in one way or another, etc., & that seeing it in this way is similar to imagining. Surely no one will deny that underlining, using parentheses can be helpful in recognizing a similarity.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#420.2) <span class="fac">[420\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/420.webp)</span> {#ts-229-420-2}

<span class="series-number">1660</span> It is clear that only the one who sees the ambiguous image as a rabbit will be able to imitate the facial expression of the rabbit. If he sees the image in _this_ way, then this will enable him to judge a certain similarity.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#420.3) <span class="fac">[420\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/420.webp)</span> {#ts-229-420-3}

<span class="series-number">1661</span> One will also only correctly estimate certain dimensions if one sees the image in _this_ way.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#420.4) <span class="fac">[420\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/420.webp)</span> {#ts-229-420-4}

<span class="series-number">1662</span> Consider that one can say: “You must hear this melody _in this way_, & then also _play_ it accordingly.”


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#420.5) <span class="fac">[420\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/420.webp)</span> {#ts-229-420-5}

<span class="series-number">1663</span> Could there not be people who cannot do mental arithmetic & cannot learn to read silently, but who are otherwise intelligent people & in no way ‘mentally deficient’?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#420.6) <span class="fac">[420\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/420.webp)</span> {#ts-229-420-6}

<span class="series-number">1664</span> There is no doubt that one often evokes an aspect through an eye movement, through a movement of the gaze.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#421.1) <span class="fac">[421\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/421.webp)</span> {#ts-229-421-1}

<span class="series-number">1665</span> But how strange! one might say – if one can discover a kind of composition – how is it possible to also _see_ it! – – How is it possible to know what one wants to say at a glance? Is this not just as remarkable?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#421.2) <span class="fac">[421\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/421.webp)</span> {#ts-229-421-2}

<span class="series-number">1666</span> Is the appearance of the aspect stranger than my memory of a particular real person, of whom I have a memory-image? Yes, there is even a similarity between the two. For one also asks here: How is it _possible_ that I have a mental image of _him_, & there is no doubt that it is _his_ image?


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#421.3) <span class="fac">[421\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/421.webp)</span> {#ts-229-421-3}

<span class="series-number">1667</span> Philosophy often solves a problem only by saying: _Here_ there is as little difficulty as _there_. Only by conjuring up a problem where there was none before. It says: “Is it not just as remarkable that ‥‥” & leaves it at that.


### [Ts-229](/ts-229/#421.4) <span class="fac">[421\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-229/421.webp)</span> {#ts-229-421-4}
